# Agent Log

## 2026-03-20T01:59:31+01:00 - XOR UNSAT presolver for Tseitin-style formulas

- Objective: reduce avoidable UNSAT search time in `satsolver.py` while keeping the submission solver correct and CLI-compliant.
- Hypothesis: detecting XOR equations encoded as full same-parity clause families will let the solver reject Tseitin-style contradictions immediately and cut the special-suite runtime without harming satisfiable cases.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py`, `tests/test_solver_regressions.py`, `out_extended.txt`, `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/satsolver_cli_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/satsolver_cli_unsat.txt`
- Benchmark results: targeted worst-case subset `43.3847s -> 35.6815s`; full 59-case suite `50.2815s -> 40.8923s`; `special/tseitin.cnf` `9.0105s -> 0.0007s` in the focused run and `9.9430s -> 0.0004s` in the full-suite report.
- Decision taken: keep the change. The optimization is backed by brute-force regression tests and materially improves the benchmark without changing output format.
- Next recommended step: instrument conflict and propagation counts on `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, and `large/test_8.cnf` to decide whether the next gain should come from branching, restarts, or learnt-clause retention.

## 2026-03-20T02:15:24+01:00 - Propagation hot-path lookup optimization

- Objective: reduce runtime on the remaining CDCL bottlenecks after the XOR presolver landed.
- Hypothesis: precomputing per-literal variable/sign/watch-index lookups and tightening the `propagate` loop will cut Python overhead in watched-literal propagation without changing solver decisions or correctness.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py`, `tools/profile_solver.py`, `out_extended.txt`, `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/satsolver_hot_unsat.txt`; `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf large/test_8.cnf medium/test_4.cnf`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_extended.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more`
- Benchmark results: targeted bottleneck set `35.6815s -> 32.7784s`; full 59-case suite `40.8923s -> 38.6404s`; `large/test_6.cnf` `17.0858s -> 16.2129s`; `special/hard.cnf` `11.2043s -> 10.6399s`; `large/test_8.cnf` `4.8993s -> 4.4817s`.
- Decision taken: keep the change. Profiling and parameter sweeps showed that restart and database thresholds were already near a local optimum, while the hot-path rewrite improved runtime with the same decisions/conflicts/propagation counts on the profiled cases.
- Next recommended step: keep focusing on propagation/data-layout wins or add a new sound structural presolver for `special/hard.cnf` and `large/test_6.cnf`, because simple restart and `next_reduce` tuning did not beat the current defaults.

## 2026-03-20T02:21:23+01:00 - Ternary clause propagation fast path

- Objective: exploit the benchmark suite's heavy 3-SAT bias to reduce propagation overhead again without changing solver behavior.
- Hypothesis: a dedicated ternary-clause branch inside `propagate` will pay off because `27,414 / 32,783` clauses in the local benchmark suite are length 3, including all clauses in the current worst offenders.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py`, `out_extended.txt`, `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/satsolver_ternary_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/satsolver_ternary_unsat.txt`; `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf large/test_8.cnf medium/test_4.cnf`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_extended.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more`
- Benchmark results: targeted bottleneck set `32.7784s -> 31.3878s`; full 59-case suite `38.6404s -> 35.9565s`; `large/test_6.cnf` `16.2129s -> 15.3465s`; `special/hard.cnf` `10.6399s -> 9.7430s`; `large/test_8.cnf` `4.4817s -> 4.0927s`.
- Decision taken: keep the change. The profiler still reports the same decisions/conflicts/processed-literal counts on the bottleneck cases, which supports the conclusion that the win came from cheaper ternary propagation work rather than heuristic drift.
- Next recommended step: continue specializing the dominant 3-SAT path with flatter ternary clause/watcher storage, while also adding stronger checker/parser regression tooling so future aggressive optimizations stay safe.

## 2026-03-20T02:44:27+01:00 - Exact-CLI benchmark mode

- Benchmark results: exact-CLI validated full-suite run `43.0869s` with `59/59` solved correctly and `0` errors; worst case `large/test_6.cnf` at `16.7068s`. The current best in-process validated snapshot remains `34.1905s`, so the exact-CLI path is about `8.8964s` slower over 59 cases, or roughly `0.1508s` per case.

## 2026-03-20T02:50:27+01:00 - Rejected broad phase-bias seeding

- Objective: test whether initializing saved phases from clause polarity bias improves the solver on the current benchmark set.
- Hypothesis: seeding the initial branch phase from `phase_bias` instead of defaulting all variables to positive will reduce search on the hard 3-SAT cases.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py` (experiment reverted; no net solver-code change), `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/polarity_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/polarity_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py /tmp/tiny_unsat.cnf /tmp/polarity_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py /tmp/tiny_unsat.cnf /tmp/polarity_unsat.txt`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver /tmp/out_phase_bias_eval.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver /tmp/bench_small_post_revert.txt small --bruteforce-var-limit 4 --cli-script satsolver.py`
- Benchmark results: targeted cases worsened overall despite one large SAT win; full validated in-process suite regressed from the current best `34.1905s` to `38.5826s`, with `large/test_6.cnf` `14.3542s -> 17.9793s`, `special/hard.cnf` `9.4234s -> 12.6070s`, `medium/test_4.cnf` `1.9977s -> 2.7649s`, while `large/test_8.cnf` improved sharply `3.9839s -> 0.2990s`.
- Decision taken: discard the change. A broad initial-phase policy helped some SAT-heavy behavior but hurt the UNSAT-heavy benchmark slices too much to keep.
- Next recommended step: if phase heuristics are revisited, do it with narrower rules or family-specific triggers; broad polarity seeding should be treated as a known regression on the current suite.

## 2026-03-20T02:57:21+01:00 - Rejected lazy heap-based branch picker

- Files changed: `satsolver.py` (experiment reverted; no net solver-code change), `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/rollback_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/rollback_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py /tmp/tiny_unsat.cnf /tmp/rollback_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py /tmp/tiny_unsat.cnf /tmp/rollback_unsat.txt`; focused timing script over `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `large/test_8.cnf`, and `medium/test_4.cnf`

## 2026-03-20T03:40:42+01:00 - Rejected stale-watcher cleanup and analyze touched-list shortcut

- Objective: reduce the remaining dense UNSAT-heavy hotspot cost without changing the solver's overall heuristics, using the repaired profiler evidence that still points to `propagate()` and `analyze()`.
- Hypothesis: immediately detaching deleted learnt clauses from watch lists during database reduction might cut stale-watcher overhead in `propagate()`, and removing the per-conflict `touched` bookkeeping from `analyze()` might reduce list churn because the tokenized `seen` array already isolates analyses safely.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py` (experiments reverted; no net solver-code change), `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; focused serial timing script over `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, and `large/test_10.cnf` before the experiments; the same focused script after each experiment; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`
- Benchmark results: the focused serial baseline for the four dense UNSAT-heavy cases was `26.1434s` (`large/test_6.cnf` `13.4447s`, `special/hard.cnf` `8.9782s`, `medium/test_4.cnf` `1.9299s`, `large/test_10.cnf` `1.7906s`). Immediate stale-watcher detachment regressed that set to `28.2716s` (`+8.14%`). Removing the `analyze()` touched-list cleanup also regressed it to `27.4386s` (`+4.95%`). A post-revert sanity rerun landed at `28.6700s`; I treated that as measurement noise under load rather than a new retained baseline because no solver change survived the cycle.
- Decision taken: discard both experiments. The repository ends this run back on the last validated solver configuration, with only new negative evidence captured in the logs.
- Next recommended step: stop chasing small bookkeeping cleanups in `reduce_database()` and `analyze()` for now; the next promising work likely needs a more structural propagation/data-layout change or a better UNSAT-targeted heuristic experiment.

The ternary-watcher split regressed that slice to `35.5294s` (`+30.50%`). Temporary instrumentation showed that reason clauses of size `2..9` accounted for about `97.22%` of all removed literals on `large/test_6.cnf` and `98.30%` on `special/hard.cnf`, but the corresponding hard cutoff in `minimize_learnt()` still regressed the same slice to `38.2439s` (`+40.50%`). - Next recommended step: keep pursuing structural propagation/data-layout changes, but do not split watcher structures or bluntly cap `minimize_learnt()` by reason-clause size. If clause minimization is revisited, it needs a much more selective rule than a hard global cutoff.

## 2026-03-20T04:18:22+01:00 - Rejected blocker watches and dense-UNSAT alternate worker

- Objective: test one more structural propagation idea and one narrower multi-worker heuristic idea that could plausibly help the remaining dense UNSAT-heavy pure-3-SAT cases.
- Hypothesis: adding blocker literals to watched-clause entries might let `propagate()` skip many clause touches when the other watched side is already satisfied, and a dense-UNSAT alternate worker using `var_decay=0.96` might be worth a narrowly gated portfolio if it improves the large dense 3-SAT outliers enough.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py` (blocker-watch experiment reverted; no net solver-code change), `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: focused serial timing script over `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, and `large/test_10.cnf` before and after the blocker-watch experiment; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle5_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle5_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle5_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle5_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; hotspot comparison runs for `var_decay=0.95` vs `0.96` on `large/test_6.cnf`, `large/test_10.cnf`, and `large/test_3.cnf`; temporary two-process wall-clock portfolio check on `large/test_6.cnf` and `large/test_10.cnf`
- Benchmark results: the focused serial baseline before the blocker-watch patch was `27.1935s` (`large/test_6.cnf` `14.3134s`, `special/hard.cnf` `9.1207s`, `medium/test_4.cnf` `1.8979s`, `large/test_10.cnf` `1.8615s`). The blocker-watch rewrite regressed that slice to `36.5950s` (`+34.57%`). The alternate `var_decay=0.96` worker was better on `large/test_6.cnf` CPU time (`18.0827s -> 17.2411s`) and `large/test_10.cnf` CPU time (`2.0791s -> 1.9824s`), but it lost on `large/test_3.cnf` (`0.3269s -> 0.3653s`) and, more importantly, did not beat real wall-clock on the big bottleneck: `large/test_6.cnf` serial `14.0442s` vs two-process portfolio `14.2078s`. It did help `large/test_10.cnf` wall-clock slightly (`1.9148s -> 1.7882s`), but not enough to justify a new gate by itself. After reverting the blocker-watch change, a sanity rerun of the four-case serial slice landed at `26.7368s`.
- Decision taken: discard both ideas. The blocker-watch rewrite was a clear regression, and the dense-UNSAT alternate worker was too narrow and inconsistent to justify extra portfolio complexity.
- Next recommended step: if portfolio work continues, it should be driven by a stronger per-family win than the small `large/test_10.cnf` gain here; otherwise the next useful work is still a more structural propagation/data-layout change that does not add watcher-entry overhead.

- Benchmark results: probing both polarities of the top `24` activity-ranked variables at the root found `0` failed literals on every measured dense case (`large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, `large/test_10.cnf`, `large/test_8.cnf`), so simple root failed-literal probing looked unpromising immediately. Otherwise, keep looking for a more structural propagation/data-layout change or a more selective conflict-analysis improvement rather than another tiny hot-path cleanup.

## 2026-03-20T04:51:26+01:00 - Rejected small-learnt minimization cutoff after watcher/minimization survey

- Objective: decide whether the next useful change should target stale watcher cleanup or a more selective `minimize_learnt()` rule before spending another cycle inside `propagate()`.
- Hypothesis: if stale watcher occupancy is materially high on the dense pure-3-SAT bottlenecks, lazy watcher cleanup might still be worth pursuing despite the earlier immediate-detachment regression; otherwise a very narrow learnt-size cutoff for clause minimization might save time by skipping low-value minimization work on short learnt clauses without repeating the earlier blunt reason-size-cutoff failure.
- Files changed: `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: watcher-occupancy survey over `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, and `large/test_10.cnf`; minimization-yield survey over the same dense UNSAT bottlenecks; temporary solver subclass sweep for `skip_len in {0, 6, 8, 10}` on `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, and `large/test_10.cnf`; repeat mixed SAT/UNSAT subset comparison for `skip_len in {0, 6}` over `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, `large/test_10.cnf`, `medium/test_3.cnf`, `large/test_8.cnf`, and `special/dense.cnf`; full 59-case measurement-only comparison for `skip_len in {0, 6}` using a temporary solver subclass.
- Benchmark results: stale watcher occupancy in solved end states was only moderate: `large/test_6.cnf` `302 / 4,948` (`6.10%`), `special/hard.cnf` `226 / 3,588` (`6.30%`), `medium/test_4.cnf` `73 / 2,595` (`2.81%`), and `large/test_10.cnf` `106 / 3,354` (`3.16%`), while binary implication lists were tiny (`34..74` entries), so stale-watcher cleanup no longer looks like the highest-value follow-up. The minimization survey showed that short learnt clauses are not free to skip, but most literal removals come from larger learnt clauses. A learnt-size cutoff at `len(learnt) <= 6` did look good on one focused four-case run (`28.3715s -> 26.3752s`), but the repeat mixed subset exposed the problem: it consistently blew up `large/test_8.cnf` from roughly `3.7..3.8s` to `13.6..14.4s`, pushing the seven-case subset from `31.9765s` to `41.3415s` on one repeat and from `30.5272s` to `39.8114s` on the next. The full 59-case measurement-only comparison also regressed badly, from `32.2658s` to `43.2184s`.
- Decision taken: discard the small-learnt minimization cutoff and keep the solver unchanged. The watcher survey also lowers the priority of more stale-watcher work unless a future measurement can show touched stale entries are much worse than the end-state occupancy suggests.
- Next recommended step: stop trying simple global minimization cutoffs by learnt size, just as earlier work ruled out a blunt reason-size cutoff. The next likely win still needs to come from a more structural propagation/data-layout change or a more selective conflict-analysis rule that can distinguish SAT-like families such as `large/test_8.cnf` from the dense UNSAT-heavy cases.

## 2026-03-20T05:03:19+01:00 - Kept lazy multiprocessing import for the portfolio path

- Objective: reduce exact-CLI submission-path overhead without changing the solver's search behavior.
- Hypothesis: importing `multiprocessing` at module import time is wasted startup cost on almost every CLI run because the portfolio gate only matches a tiny subset of local formulas; moving that import behind `solve_cnf_portfolio()` should improve exact-CLI benchmark time while leaving solver decisions unchanged.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py`, `out_extended.txt`, `out_cli_extended.txt`, `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle9_portfolio_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle9_portfolio_sat.txt`; repeated import-timing script using `python -c 'import satsolver'`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver /tmp/pre_cli_small.txt large --bruteforce-var-limit 0 --cli-script satsolver.py`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver /tmp/post_cli_large.txt large --bruteforce-var-limit 0 --cli-script satsolver.py`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver /tmp/post_cli_full.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16 --cli-script satsolver.py`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver /tmp/post_inprocess_full.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16`
- Benchmark results: the current portfolio gate still matches exactly one local benchmark case, `large/test_8.cnf`, and repeated single-case timings showed the serial bias-seeded path and the current portfolio are already very close (`0.2401..0.2673s` vs `0.2605..0.2739s`). Lazy-loading `multiprocessing` cut import time noticeably: mean `0.03461s -> 0.02719s`, median `0.03184s -> 0.02248s`. The validated exact-CLI `large/` run improved from `17.6655s` to `17.3190s`, with `large/test_8.cnf` `0.3306s -> 0.3206s`. The validated exact-CLI full suite improved from `33.6033s` to `33.0018s`, still with `59/59` cases correct and `0` errors. A fresh validated in-process full-suite run also landed at `29.4438s`; I recorded that snapshot, but I treat the CLI improvement as the primary signal here because the code change targets startup rather than core search.
- Decision taken: keep the change. It is low-risk, keeps the portfolio behavior intact, and improves the exact required CLI path by removing startup cost from runs that never touch multiprocessing.
- Next recommended step: return to solver-core work for the next cycle. If portfolio logic is revisited later, remember that the local gate currently matches only `large/test_8.cnf`, so any change there needs to beat the current lazy-imported path on multi-run exact-CLI evidence, not just one favorable timing sample.

## 2026-03-20T05:14:57+01:00 - Kept per-literal truth-value cache in propagation

- Objective: reduce propagation overhead on the dense UNSAT-heavy pure-3-SAT bottlenecks without changing the solver's search path.
- Hypothesis: repeatedly computing `values[literal_var[literal]] * literal_sign[literal]` in `propagate()` and root simplification is avoidable overhead in Python; maintaining a direct per-literal truth-value cache should make the hot path cheaper while preserving decisions and conflicts.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py`, `tests/test_solver_regressions.py`, `out_extended.txt`, `out_cli_extended.txt`, `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle11_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle11_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle11_large8.txt`; `python tools/checker.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle11_large8.txt`; `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf medium/test_4.cnf` before and after the change; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver /tmp/post_cycle11_inproc_full_seq.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver /tmp/post_cycle11_cli_full_seq.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16 --cli-script satsolver.py`
- Benchmark results: the profiled dense UNSAT slice improved from `30.2643s` to `29.5402s` (`-2.39%`): `large/test_6.cnf` `16.8129s -> 16.4005s`, `special/hard.cnf` `11.2271s -> 10.9486s`, `medium/test_4.cnf` `2.2243s -> 2.1911s`. Decisions, conflicts, and processed-literal counts stayed identical on all three cases, so the gain is real hot-path overhead reduction. The validated in-process 59-case suite improved from `28.7549s` to `27.9763s`, still with `59/59` cases correct. The validated exact-CLI 59-case suite improved from `31.5073s` to `30.5932s`, also with `59/59` cases correct and `0` errors.
- Decision taken: keep the change. It is a small structural propagation win with preserved search behavior, and the added regression test helps protect the new cache against future assignment/backtrack bugs.
- Next recommended step: keep pushing solver-core work on the dense UNSAT bottlenecks, but move farther downstream than simple lookup elimination. The next likely wins are either cheaper watched-clause traversal on the ternary-heavy path or more selective conflict-analysis/minimization work that does not repeat the earlier global-cutoff regressions.

## 2026-03-20T05:23:14+01:00 - Kept in-place learnt minimization plus merged learnt finalization

- Objective: reduce conflict-analysis overhead on the dense UNSAT-heavy bottlenecks without changing the solver's search path.
- Hypothesis: `analyze()` is still the second-largest hotspot after `propagate()`, and its tail currently does extra work by allocating a new minimized learnt list and then scanning the final clause twice more to pick the backtrack literal and compute the LBD. Compacting in place and merging those final scans should shave per-conflict overhead while preserving learnt clauses and search counts.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py`, `out_extended.txt`, `out_cli_extended.txt`, `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle12_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle12_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle12_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle12_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; `python satsolver.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle12_large8.txt`; `python tools/checker.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle12_large8.txt`; `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf medium/test_4.cnf` before and after the change; `python - <<'PY' ... cProfile solve_cnf_serial on large/test_6.cnf before and after ... PY`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver /tmp/post_cycle12_inproc_clean.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver /tmp/post_cycle12_cli_clean.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16 --cli-script satsolver.py`
- Benchmark results: the profiled dense UNSAT slice improved from `31.7000s` to `29.6050s` (`-6.61%`): `large/test_6.cnf` `17.7136s -> 16.5008s`, `special/hard.cnf` `11.7149s -> 10.9579s`, `medium/test_4.cnf` `2.2715s -> 2.1463s`. Decisions, conflicts, and processed-literal counts stayed identical on all three cases, so the gain is real overhead reduction. On `large/test_6.cnf`, the `cProfile` snapshot moved `analyze()` from `5.987s` to `5.356s` and `minimize_learnt()` from `0.933s` to `0.708s`. The validated in-process 59-case suite improved from `27.9763s` to `27.3023s`, still with `59/59` cases correct, and the validated exact-CLI 59-case suite improved from `30.5932s` to `30.1080s`, again with `59/59` correct and `0` errors.
- Decision taken: keep the change. It is a contained conflict-analysis optimization with preserved search behavior and a measurable full-suite win in both benchmark modes.
- Next recommended step: continue on the remaining dense UNSAT bottlenecks, but now look past the obvious learnt-tail cleanup. The next promising area is deeper watched-clause traversal cost inside `propagate()` or a more selective minimization/conflict-analysis rule that is less blunt than the already-rejected global cutoffs.

## 2026-03-20T05:32:44+01:00 - Rejected keeping ternary learnt clauses during database reduction

- Objective: test whether the remaining dense UNSAT-heavy 3-SAT bottlenecks would benefit from retaining ternary learnt clauses longer during `reduce_database()`.
- Hypothesis: because the hardest remaining cases are pure 3-SAT UNSAT families, preserving learnt clauses of length `3` in addition to binaries might keep more useful short deductions alive, reduce future conflicts, and outperform the current `len <= 2 or lbd <= 2` retention rule.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py` (experiment reverted; no net solver-code change), `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle13_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle13_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle13_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle13_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; serial timing script over `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, `large/test_10.cnf`, and `large/test_8.cnf` before and after the temporary rule; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v` after revert; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle13_revert_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle13_revert_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle13_revert_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle13_revert_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; `python satsolver.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle13_revert_large8.txt`; `python tools/checker.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle13_revert_large8.txt`; post-revert serial sanity timing on `large/test_6.cnf` and `large/test_8.cnf`
- Benchmark results: the four dense UNSAT cases did improve under the temporary rule, from `25.9122s` to `24.3444s` (`-6.05%`): `large/test_6.cnf` `13.3445s -> 14.2395s`, `special/hard.cnf` `8.8621s -> 6.5355s`, `medium/test_4.cnf` `1.9022s -> 2.1911s`, `large/test_10.cnf` `1.8034s -> 1.3783s`. But the SAT-like `large/test_8.cnf` serial path exploded from `3.7121s` to `29.3577s`, pushing the five-case mixed slice from `29.6243s` to `53.7021s` (`+81.28%`). After reverting, quick serial sanity timings returned to `large/test_6.cnf` `13.2304s` and `large/test_8.cnf` `3.5765s`.
- Decision taken: discard the heuristic and keep the solver on the previous retained baseline. Clause size alone is not a safe retention classifier here; preserving all ternary learnt clauses is too favorable to some dense UNSAT families and disastrous on SAT-like cases.
- Next recommended step: if clause-retention policy is revisited, do not gate it on learnt size alone. Future retention work needs a better classifier than clause length, or it should stay focused on propagation-layout cost instead of broad database-policy changes.

## 2026-03-20T05:41:11+01:00 - Rejected in-place watcher-list compaction in propagate

- Objective: reduce list-mutation overhead in the propagation hot path without changing solver behavior.
- Hypothesis: replacing the current swap-and-pop watcher removal pattern with a read/write compaction pass should cut `list.pop()` churn in `propagate()`, preserve the watched-clause semantics, and improve the dense UNSAT-heavy 3-SAT cases.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py` (experiment reverted; no net solver-code change), `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: baseline serial timing script over `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, `large/test_10.cnf`, and `large/test_8.cnf`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle14_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle14_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle14_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle14_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; `python satsolver.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle14_large8.txt`; `python tools/checker.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle14_large8.txt`; `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf medium/test_4.cnf` under the experimental solver; experimental serial timing script over `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, `large/test_10.cnf`, and `large/test_8.cnf`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v` after revert; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle14_revert_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle14_revert_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle14_revert_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle14_revert_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; `python satsolver.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle14_revert_large8.txt`; `python tools/checker.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle14_revert_large8.txt`; post-revert serial sanity timing on `large/test_6.cnf` and `large/test_8.cnf`; post-revert `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf medium/test_4.cnf`
- Benchmark results: the experimental watcher compaction regressed the dense four-case serial slice from `25.7604s` to `28.8592s` (`+12.03%`) and the mixed five-case slice from `29.3953s` to `37.3648s` (`+27.11%`). The biggest individual losses were `special/hard.cnf` `8.6575s -> 10.7790s`, `large/test_10.cnf` `1.7634s -> 2.2581s`, and `large/test_8.cnf` `3.6349s -> 8.5056s`. The profiler showed that this was not a pure bookkeeping change: `large/test_6.cnf` shifted from the retained baseline `72,886` decisions / `59,201` conflicts / `2,431,409` processed literals to `72,629` / `58,977` / `2,404,568`, `special/hard.cnf` shifted from `54,245` / `44,619` / `1,737,825` to `65,618` / `53,590` / `2,076,568`, and `medium/test_4.cnf` shifted from `13,510` / `10,995` / `396,463` to `11,204` / `9,056` / `325,604`. After reverting, serial sanity checks returned to `large/test_6.cnf` `13.0119s` and `large/test_8.cnf` `3.7330s`, and the post-revert profiler returned to the retained search counts.
- Decision taken: discard the change and keep the previous retained baseline. Even though the rewrite preserved satisfiability correctness, the different watcher traversal order was enough to perturb propagation order and hurt overall search.
- Next recommended step: if watcher-list work is revisited, preserve the effective traversal behavior much more closely. The next better target still looks like a propagation/data-layout change that does not reorder watcher handling, or a more selective conflict-analysis rule.

- Files changed: `satsolver.py` (experiment reverted; no net solver-code change), `tests/test_solver_regressions.py` (temporary sync regression reverted; no net test change), `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`

That is only about `-0.52%..+0.14%` on the dense four-case slice and `-0.11%..+0.14%` on the mixed five-case slice, which is not a trustworthy improvement signal for a more complex data layout.
- Decision taken: discard the change and keep the simpler retained baseline. The shadow arrays reduced Python-level overhead, but not enough to produce a strong, repeatable solver-level speedup.
- Next recommended step: if clause-data layout is revisited, it should be with a more direct representation that removes `Clause` indirection outright on the hot path, not by mirroring object state in parallel arrays. The next better target still looks like a structural propagation/conflict-analysis change with a larger expected payoff.

- Objective: reduce the large raw `append()` / `pop()` cost in the search loop without changing solver heuristics or watcher order.
- Hypothesis: the trail only needs to grow to `num_vars`, so replacing repeated `trail.append()` / `trail.pop()` with an active `trail_size` index and slot reuse should eliminate most list-growth churn after the initial fill, make `backtrack()` cheaper, and improve the dense UNSAT-heavy pure-3-SAT bottlenecks.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py` (experiment reverted; no net solver-code change), `tools/profile_solver.py` (temporary `max_trail` / decision-path updates reverted; no net tool change), `tests/test_solver_regressions.py` (temporary trail-reuse regression reverted; no net test change), `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`

- Benchmark results: the active-trail rewrite did reduce some of the raw list bookkeeping in the profiled trace on `large/test_6.cnf`: list `append()` calls dropped from `13.08M` to `9.71M`, list `pop()` calls dropped from `10.11M` to `6.75M`, and `backtrack()` dropped from `2.090s` to `1.045s`. But that saving was overwhelmed by a slower propagation path: `propagate()` worsened from `14.033s` to `14.615s`, and the clean five-case wall-clock slice regressed from the clean reverted baseline `dense4_total=26.3783s` / `mixed5_total=30.1683s` to the experimental `30.4033s` / `34.2696s` (`+15.26%` and `+13.59%`). The profiled dense UNSAT slice regressed too: baseline `16.6956s + 11.5282s + 2.3187s = 30.5425s` versus experimental `17.6567s + 12.8892s + 2.3702s = 32.9161s` (`+7.77%`), with decisions, conflicts, and processed-literal counts unchanged.
- Decision taken: discard the change and keep the previous retained baseline. Cutting `append()` / `pop()` was not enough; the active-trail indexing made the dominant propagation loop slower overall.
- Next recommended step: do not revisit active trail-size indexing that keeps stale trail entries in place. The next meaningful win still looks like a structural propagation or conflict-analysis improvement, but not one that trades simpler active-list semantics for a noisier index-based trail path.

## 2026-03-20T06:25:22+01:00 - Rejected globally protecting LBD<=3 learnt clauses

- Objective: revisit clause-retention policy with a better classifier than learnt size alone, and test whether globally protecting `LBD<=3` learnt clauses helps the remaining dense UNSAT-heavy 3-SAT bottlenecks.
- Hypothesis: the hard UNSAT cases keep a large population of useful `LBD=3` clauses alive even under the current policy, so preventing `reduce_database()` from ever deleting them might reduce future conflicts enough to outweigh a larger live learnt database, while avoiding the family-specific blowup seen when all ternary clauses were protected.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py` (experiment reverted; no net solver-code change), `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: inspection scripts for live/deleted learnt-clause `lbd` distributions and reduction snapshots on `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, and `large/test_8.cnf`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle18_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle18_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle18_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle18_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; `python satsolver.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle18_large8.txt`; `python tools/checker.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle18_large8.txt`; clean experimental five-case serial timing script over `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, `large/test_10.cnf`, and `large/test_8.cnf`; experimental `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf medium/test_4.cnf`; experimental `cProfile` on `large/test_6.cnf`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v` after revert; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle18_revert_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle18_revert_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle18_revert_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle18_revert_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; `python satsolver.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle18_revert_large8.txt`; `python tools/checker.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle18_revert_large8.txt`; clean post-revert five-case serial timing script; clean post-revert `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf medium/test_4.cnf`
- Benchmark results: the exploratory inspection did confirm that `LBD=3` clauses are a major live cohort on the hard UNSAT families, and that some `LBD=3` clauses are still being deleted under the current policy. The temporary `LBD<=3` keep rule improved the search counts on the dense UNSAT slice, but it grew the live learnt database dramatically: on the profile run, `large/test_6.cnf` went from `970` live learnts to `2624`, `special/hard.cnf` from `874` to `2067`, and `medium/test_4.cnf` from `401` to `1110`. Wall-clock performance regressed badly overall. The clean five-case serial slice moved from the restored baseline `dense4_total=26.8256s` / `mixed5_total=30.5189s` to the experimental `28.7174s` / `34.8344s` (`+7.05%` and `+14.14%`). The profiled dense UNSAT slice also regressed from `17.0456s + 11.3660s + 2.2919s = 30.7035s` to `27.8761s + 11.9098s + 1.9587s = 41.7446s` (`+35.96%`), and `large/test_8.cnf` was the main casualty on the serial slice (`3.6933s -> 6.1171s`, `+65.63%`).
- Decision taken: discard the change and keep the previous retained baseline. `LBD<=3` is still too blunt a global retention rule here; it helps some UNSAT families at the search-tree level but expands the learnt database enough to lose badly overall, especially on SAT-like `large/test_8.cnf`.
- Next recommended step: if clause-retention policy is revisited again, do not use a global `LBD<=3` protection rule. Future retention work needs a case classifier stronger than just LBD threshold, or it should stay focused on cheaper propagation/conflict-analysis rather than a broader live-learnt database.

## 2026-03-20T06:37:55+01:00 - Rejected caching clause length inside Clause objects

- Objective: reduce the remaining `len(clause.lits)` overhead inside `propagate()`, `attach_clause()`, and `reduce_database()` without duplicating watcher state or changing traversal order.
- Hypothesis: storing a cached `size` slot directly on each `Clause` and reading that in the hot path should keep the simpler object layout, slash builtin `len()` traffic, and produce a measurable wall-clock win on the dense pure-3-SAT bottlenecks.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py` (experiment reverted; no net solver-code change), `out_extended.txt`, `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: fresh clean serial timing script over `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, `large/test_10.cnf`, and `large/test_8.cnf`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle19_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle19_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle19_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle19_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; `python satsolver.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle19_large8.txt`; `python tools/checker.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle19_large8.txt`; clean experimental five-case serial timing script twice; experimental `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf medium/test_4.cnf`; experimental `cProfile` on `large/test_6.cnf`; validated full-suite benchmark `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_extended.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v` after revert; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle19_revert_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle19_revert_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle19_revert_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle19_revert_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; `python satsolver.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle19_revert_large8.txt`; `python tools/checker.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle19_revert_large8.txt`; post-revert serial sanity timing on `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, and `large/test_8.cnf`
- Benchmark results: the Python-level profile looked much better. On `large/test_6.cnf`, `cProfile` total function calls dropped from `60,213,022` to `48,171,759`, builtin `len()` calls dropped from `18,646,469` to `6,605,206`, and `propagate()` `tottime` dropped from `13.606s` to `11.863s` under `cProfile`. The profiled dense UNSAT slice also improved slightly overall, from `17.0944s + 11.4704s + 2.2529s = 30.8177s` to `17.3009s + 11.1281s + 2.1767s = 30.6057s` (`-0.69%`), with decisions, conflicts, and processed-literal counts unchanged. But the clean wall-clock evidence stayed too weak and unstable to justify keeping the extra metadata: the first clean five-case run moved from `dense4_total=26.6121s` / `mixed5_total=30.1984s` to `26.7220s` / `30.5350s`, and the validated full 59-case suite under the experimental solver landed at `28.0041s`, which is slower than the retained best-known `27.3023s`. After reverting, post-revert sanity timings returned to `large/test_6.cnf` `14.2554s`, `special/hard.cnf` `8.8604s`, and `large/test_8.cnf` `3.9115s`.
- Decision taken: discard the change and keep the previous retained baseline. Lower `len()` traffic and better `cProfile` numbers were not enough to prove a reliable end-to-end win, and the validated full suite regressed.
- Next recommended step: do not revisit a cached `Clause.size` field by itself. If clause-layout work is revisited, it needs a representation that produces a clearer solver-level wall-clock gain than a length cache alone.

## 2026-03-20T06:48:40+01:00 - Rejected tuple-based blocker-literal watch entries

- Objective: reduce expensive clause touches inside watched-literal propagation on the ternary-heavy hotspot set without changing the clause database or branching heuristics.
- Hypothesis: storing watcher entries as `(clause_id, blocker_literal)` pairs and checking the blocker before touching the clause should skip a meaningful fraction of satisfied watches, making `propagate()` cheaper on the dense pure-3-SAT bottlenecks.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py` (experiment reverted; no net solver-code change), `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: fresh clean serial timing script over `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, `large/test_10.cnf`, and `large/test_8.cnf`; `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf medium/test_4.cnf` on the retained baseline; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle20_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle20_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle20_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle20_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; `python satsolver.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle20_large8.txt`; `python tools/checker.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle20_large8.txt`; clean experimental five-case serial timing script; experimental `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf medium/test_4.cnf`; experimental `cProfile` on `large/test_6.cnf`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v` after revert; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle20_revert_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle20_revert_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle20_revert_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle20_revert_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; `python satsolver.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle20_revert_large8.txt`; `python tools/checker.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle20_revert_large8.txt`; post-revert serial sanity timing on `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, and `large/test_8.cnf`
- Benchmark results: the change was a clear loss. The clean five-case serial slice regressed from baseline `dense4_total=27.6382s` / `mixed5_total=31.6482s` to `36.8277s` / `38.7381s` (`+33.25%` and `+22.40%`). It helped the SAT-like `large/test_8.cnf` path (`4.0100s -> 1.9104s`) but badly hurt the dense UNSAT cases: `large/test_6.cnf` `14.1359s -> 20.2104s`, `special/hard.cnf` `9.4415s -> 11.9502s`, and `medium/test_4.cnf` `2.0564s -> 2.8121s`. The profiled dense UNSAT slice regressed from `16.3990s + 11.4599s + 2.2471s = 30.1060s` to `24.9045s + 15.1410s + 3.1649s = 43.2104s` (`+43.53%`). Search counts changed sharply rather than staying flat: on `large/test_6.cnf`, decisions / conflicts / processed literals jumped from `72,886 / 59,201 / 2,431,409` to `89,078 / 72,547 / 2,962,836`. The experimental `cProfile` on `large/test_6.cnf` worsened to `42.821s` total with `73,480,592` calls, and `propagate()` alone rose to `20.065s`.
- Decision taken: discard the change and keep the previous retained baseline. This blocker-watch representation did not just add tuple overhead; it perturbed propagation enough to make the hard UNSAT search path much worse overall.
- Next recommended step: do not revisit tuple-based blocker-literal watch entries that defer stale watcher cleanup and change watch-list payloads in pure Python. If blocker ideas come back later, they need a materially different layout and stronger evidence that they will not destabilize the dense UNSAT families.

- Tests executed: fresh retained-baseline serial timing script over `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, `large/test_10.cnf`, and `large/test_8.cnf`; fresh retained-baseline `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf medium/test_4.cnf`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle21_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle21_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle21_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle21_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; `python satsolver.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle21_large8.txt`; `python tools/checker.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle21_large8.txt`; clean experimental five-case serial timing script twice; experimental `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf medium/test_4.cnf`; experimental `cProfile` on `large/test_6.cnf`; validated full-suite benchmark `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_extended.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v` after revert; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle21_revert_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle21_revert_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle21_revert_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle21_revert_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; `python satsolver.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle21_revert_large8.txt`; `python tools/checker.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle21_revert_large8.txt`; post-revert serial sanity timing on `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, and `large/test_8.cnf`
- Benchmark results: the solver behavior stayed identical on the profiled dense UNSAT slice, and that slice improved modestly: `17.0666s + 11.5015s + 2.3811s = 30.9492s` on the retained baseline versus `16.8734s + 11.1525s + 2.3136s = 30.3395s` under the experiment (`-1.97%`). But the stronger signal lost: the validated 59-case full suite under the experimental solver landed at `28.2906s`, slower than the retained best-known `27.3023s`, with the worst case moving to `13.2171s` on `large/test_6.cnf`. The experimental `cProfile` on `large/test_6.cnf` also did not show a compelling Python-level win, landing at `32.529s` total with `propagate()` at `14.453s`.
- Decision taken: discard the change and keep the previous retained baseline. Removing `Clause` objects outright still was not enough to beat the validated full-suite baseline, so that data-layout direction does not currently justify the refactor cost.
- Next recommended step: do not revisit raw-literal clause storage with simple parallel learnt/activity/lbd/deleted arrays by itself. Future structural work should target a more specific propagation or conflict-analysis bottleneck than “remove Clause objects” alone.

## 2026-03-20T07:12:33+01:00 - Kept repeat-capable validated benchmarking

- Objective: reduce timing noise in benchmark-driven solver work by making the benchmark harness repeat-capable and recording repeat-aware baseline artifacts for both in-process and exact-CLI modes.
- Hypothesis: if `benchmark_suite.py` can run each case multiple times and report per-case samples plus median representative times, future keep/revert decisions will be more trustworthy than one-off full-suite timings alone.
- Files changed: `benchmark_suite.py`, `tests/test_benchmark_suite.py`, `out_extended.txt`, `out_cli_extended.txt`, `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_extended.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16 --repeat 2`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_cli_extended.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16 --cli-script satsolver.py --repeat 2`
- Benchmark results: `benchmark_suite.py` now supports `--repeat N`, emits per-case `samples=[...]`, and reports median representative totals plus `measured_total` across all repeats. The validated in-process 59-case `--repeat 2` run solved `59/59` correctly with representative total `28.9770s`, measured total `57.9540s`, wall clock `58.1751s`, and worst case `large/test_6.cnf` at `13.4683s` from samples `[13.2856, 13.6510]`. The validated exact-CLI `--repeat 2` run also solved `59/59` correctly with representative total `31.5848s`, measured total `63.1696s`, wall clock `63.3896s`, and worst case `large/test_6.cnf` at `13.8780s` from `[14.0086, 13.7474]`. An early fixed-phase worker resweep also reconfirmed there is no simple alternate worker to add right now on the dense five-case slice: default `29.0347s`, `phase_bias` `30.7907s`, `all_true` `29.8937s`, `all_false` `43.1369s`.
- Decision taken: keep the harness change. It does not alter solver behavior, strengthens measurement quality, and leaves behind repeat-aware benchmark artifacts while preserving the earlier best-known single-run solver snapshots for apples-to-apples history.
- Next recommended step: use `benchmark_suite.py --repeat 2` as the default confirmation pass for future solver-core candidates, and keep the next optimization effort focused on structural `propagate()` / conflict-analysis work on the dense UNSAT bottlenecks rather than revisiting fixed-phase alternate workers.

## 2026-03-20T07:28:40+01:00 - Kept deeper propagation and minimization profiling

- Objective: sharpen the next solver-core target by extending the profiler from high-level counts to concrete watched-clause and learnt-minimization workload breakdowns.
- Hypothesis: if `tools/profile_solver.py` reports binary-vs-watch activity, ternary-vs-generic watch visits, relocation/unit/conflict outcomes, and learnt-clause shrinkage, it will tell us whether the next win should come from watched-clause traversal or from reducing learnt-clause length.
- Files changed: `tools/profile_solver.py`, `tests/test_profile_solver.py`, `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf medium/test_4.cnf`
- Benchmark results: the upgraded profiler now reports watched-clause workload and minimization shrinkage. On `large/test_6.cnf`, the hard UNSAT path is overwhelmingly watch-driven: `13,435,147` watch visits versus only `11,953` binary checks, with `7,904,849` ternary watch visits, `1,939,491` large-clause watch visits, `3,474,649` satisfied skips, `6,556,559` watch relocations, and `3,228,584` watch-driven unit propagations; deleted-watch skips are only `116,158`. `special/hard.cnf` shows the same shape: `9,550,550` watch visits, `14,395` binary checks, `4,427,121` ternary visits, `1,725,097` large visits, `3,311,070` satisfied skips, `3,919,510` relocations, and `2,188,116` watch-driven units. Clause minimization is still doing real work on all three bottlenecks: average learnt length shrinks from `14.31 -> 11.87` on `large/test_6.cnf`, `13.80 -> 11.31` on `special/hard.cnf`, and `12.51 -> 10.29` on `medium/test_4.cnf`, removing `144,647`, `111,028`, and `24,351` literals respectively across the runs.
- Decision taken: keep the profiler upgrade. The solver remains unchanged, but the repo now has direct evidence that binary-path and stale-deleted-watch work are secondary, while watch traversal plus relocation/unit handling dominate the remaining UNSAT bottlenecks.
- Next recommended step: focus the next solver-core attempt on the watched-clause path itself, especially ternary and generic relocation/unit handling or conflict-analysis changes that shorten learnt clauses further without using blunt global cutoffs.

## 2026-03-20T07:34:25+01:00 - Rejected recursive learnt-clause minimization

- Objective: test whether a stronger recursive redundancy check in conflict analysis can shrink learnt clauses enough to offset its extra Python work on the dense UNSAT bottlenecks.
- Hypothesis: because the upgraded profiler showed minimization still removes many literals on the hard UNSAT cases, replacing the current one-hop redundancy test with recursive reason traversal might cut future watch traffic enough to beat its added per-conflict cost.
- Files changed: `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: `python - <<'PY' ... baseline vs RecursiveMinSolver over large/test_6.cnf, special/hard.cnf, medium/test_4.cnf, large/test_10.cnf, large/test_8.cnf ... PY`
- Benchmark results: the recursive minimization experiment was a clear regression on the focused serial slice. Baseline totals were `30.0874s` overall with `large/test_6.cnf 13.3046s`, `special/hard.cnf 8.9443s`, `medium/test_4.cnf 1.8355s`, `large/test_10.cnf 1.6580s`, and `large/test_8.cnf 4.3450s`. The recursive version regressed to `35.9624s` overall: `large/test_6.cnf 14.8008s`, `special/hard.cnf 11.7288s`, `medium/test_4.cnf 2.3683s`, `large/test_10.cnf 1.5048s`, and `large/test_8.cnf 5.5598s`. That is `+19.54%` overall, with the biggest losses on `special/hard.cnf` and `large/test_8.cnf`.
- Decision taken: discard the idea and keep the retained solver baseline unchanged. A naive Python implementation of recursive redundancy checking is too expensive on this workload even if it may shrink some learnt clauses further.
- Next recommended step: if clause minimization is revisited again, it needs to be cheaper or more selective than full recursive redundancy traversal in Python; otherwise stay focused on watched-clause traversal and relocation/unit handling.

## 2026-03-20T07:41:04+01:00 - Kept finer watch-path outcome and probe-depth profiling

- Objective: sharpen the next propagation experiment by separating ternary-vs-large watch outcomes and measuring how deep large-clause replacement scans actually go.
- Hypothesis: if most remaining watched-clause work is still ternary relocation/unit handling and large-clause scans are only a few probes deep on average, the next solver-core attempt should target the ternary fast path first instead of another broad large-clause data-layout rewrite.
- Files changed: `tools/profile_solver.py`, `tests/test_profile_solver.py`, `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf medium/test_4.cnf large/test_8.cnf`
- Benchmark results: the profiler now breaks watched-clause work into ternary-vs-large relocations, units, conflicts, and large-clause probe depth. On `large/test_6.cnf`, ternary outcomes dominate the remaining work: `4,813,494` ternary relocations, `3,037,579` ternary units, and `53,776` ternary conflicts versus `1,743,065` large relocations, `191,005` large units, and `5,421` large conflicts. The large-clause scan itself is not especially deep there, averaging only `2.36` probes overall, `2.22` on successful relocation, and `3.61` on failure, with max depth `28`. `special/hard.cnf` and `medium/test_4.cnf` show the same shape (`avg_large_probe` `2.32` and `2.56`). `large/test_8.cnf` is the interesting contrast case: it still spends much more time in ternary watches than large ones (`2,045,418` vs `336,168` visits), but its large-clause scans are somewhat deeper (`avg_large_probe=3.39`, `avg_large_probe_failure=4.85`, `max_large_probe=38`) and its learnt clauses stay longer on average (`18.51 -> 16.34`, `avg_lbd=11.08`).
- Decision taken: keep the profiler/test upgrade and leave the production solver unchanged. The new breakdown is strong enough to rule out another blind large-clause scan rewrite as the immediate next move.
- Next recommended step: target the ternary relocation/unit path first with a change that preserves watch traversal order; only revisit large-clause scan optimization if it is selective enough to help SAT-like `large/test_8.cnf` without duplicating mutable watch state.

## 2026-03-20T07:47:53+01:00 - Rejected eager ternary watch relocation on already-satisfied clauses

- Objective: test whether the ternary fast path can cut future satisfied re-visits by moving the false watch to the third literal even when the other watched literal is already `TRUE`.
- Hypothesis: because ternary watch traffic dominates the hard UNSAT cases and the third-literal check is cheap, relocating off the false watched literal eagerly on satisfied ternary clauses might reduce future watched-clause churn enough to beat the extra immediate relocation work.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py` (experiment reverted; no net solver-code change), `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: baseline serial five-case timing script with `SATSOLVER_DISABLE_PORTFOLIO=1`; experimental serial five-case timing script over `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, `large/test_10.cnf`, and `large/test_8.cnf`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v` after revert; clean post-revert serial five-case timing script
- Benchmark results: the idea regressed badly. The clean serial five-case baseline was `28.4768s` total with `large/test_6.cnf 12.6142s`, `special/hard.cnf 8.7291s`, `medium/test_4.cnf 1.7953s`, `large/test_10.cnf 1.7525s`, and `large/test_8.cnf 3.5857s`. The experimental solver slowed to `36.4731s`: `large/test_6.cnf 17.9193s`, `special/hard.cnf 8.1042s`, `medium/test_4.cnf 2.1350s`, `large/test_10.cnf 1.8333s`, and `large/test_8.cnf 6.4813s`. After reverting, the same serial slice returned to `28.7496s` with `large/test_6.cnf 12.9485s`, `special/hard.cnf 8.5619s`, `medium/test_4.cnf 1.8272s`, `large/test_10.cnf 1.7770s`, and `large/test_8.cnf 3.6350s`.
- Decision taken: discard the change and keep the retained solver baseline unchanged. Eagerly relocating ternary watches away from already-satisfied clauses increases churn instead of reducing it, and it is especially harmful on the SAT-like `large/test_8.cnf` family.
- Next recommended step: keep ternary watch work focused on cheaper relocation/unit handling that does not force extra motion on already-satisfied clauses; if satisfied-skip work is revisited again, it needs a stronger selector than “other watch is already true”.

## 2026-03-20T07:58:50+01:00 - Kept inlined propagation-unit assignment path

- Objective: cut hot-loop overhead on the millions of watch-driven and binary-driven unit propagations without changing watch traversal order or solver heuristics.
- Hypothesis: when propagation has already proved a literal is `UNASSIGNED`, inlining the assignment updates directly inside `propagate()` should be cheaper than calling `enqueue()` millions of times, while leaving decisions, conflicts, and processed-literal counts unchanged.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py`, `tools/profile_solver.py`, `tests/test_solver_regressions.py`, `out_extended.txt`, `out_cli_extended.txt`, `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: baseline serial five-case timing script with `SATSOLVER_DISABLE_PORTFOLIO=1`; experimental serial five-case timing script over `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, `large/test_10.cnf`, and `large/test_8.cnf`; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/cycle_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/cycle_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; `python satsolver.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle_large8.txt`; `python tools/checker.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle_large8.txt`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf medium/test_4.cnf large/test_8.cnf`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_extended.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_extended.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16 --repeat 2`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_cli_extended.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16 --cli-script satsolver.py`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_cli_extended.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16 --cli-script satsolver.py --repeat 2`; final exact-CLI rerun `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_cli_extended.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16 --cli-script satsolver.py`
- Benchmark results: the serial five-case hotspot slice improved from the restored baseline `28.7496s` to `27.1753s` (`-5.47%`): `large/test_6.cnf 12.9485s -> 12.1792s`, `special/hard.cnf 8.5619s -> 8.1314s`, `medium/test_4.cnf 1.8272s -> 1.7000s`, `large/test_10.cnf 1.7770s -> 1.6590s`, and `large/test_8.cnf 3.6350s -> 3.5058s`. The upgraded profiler confirmed the search path stayed identical on the main hotspots while runtime dropped: `large/test_6.cnf` remained at `72,886` decisions, `59,201` conflicts, and `2,431,409` processed literals but fell from `20.8801s` to `18.8837s`; `special/hard.cnf` fell from `13.9615s` to `12.2556s`; `medium/test_4.cnf` from `2.8093s` to `2.5129s`; and `large/test_8.cnf` from `5.3621s` to `4.8326s`. The validated full suites also improved strongly: in-process single-run `27.3023s -> 26.4178s`, in-process `--repeat 2` representative `28.9770s -> 26.4508s`, exact-CLI single-run `30.1080s -> 28.4550s`, and exact-CLI `--repeat 2` representative `31.5848s -> 28.7122s`, all still `59/59` correct.
- Decision taken: keep the change. This is a real hot-loop overhead reduction with preserved search counts, clean validation, and broad benchmark wins in both in-process and exact-CLI measurement modes.
- Next recommended step: the obvious `enqueue()` call overhead on already-known-unassigned propagation units is now addressed. The next meaningful gain still looks like reducing watched-clause traversal itself before the unit/conflict handoff, especially in the ternary relocation/unit path and downstream conflict analysis.

## 2026-03-20T08:05:57+01:00 - Rejected encoded watch-slot payloads

- Tests executed: checked `maybe.md` for the stdlib-parallelism reminder; out-of-tree same-run timing script comparing the retained serial solver against a temporary `SideWatchSolver` subclass with encoded watch-slot payloads on `large/test_6.cnf`, `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_4.cnf`, `large/test_10.cnf`, and `large/test_8.cnf`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle_large8_verify.txt`; `python tools/checker.py large/test_8.cnf /tmp/cycle_large8_verify.txt`
- Benchmark results: the encoded-payload prototype lost on the same-run serial five-case slice. The retained baseline came in at `27.2088s` total with `large/test_6.cnf 12.3262s`, `special/hard.cnf 8.1615s`, `medium/test_4.cnf 1.6892s`, `large/test_10.cnf 1.6469s`, and `large/test_8.cnf 3.3849s`. The temporary `SideWatchSolver` regressed to `27.7040s` total: `large/test_6.cnf 12.3438s`, `special/hard.cnf 8.3617s`, `medium/test_4.cnf 1.7474s`, `large/test_10.cnf 1.7169s`, and `large/test_8.cnf 3.5342s`. That is a `+1.82%` regression overall, with no compensating family win.
- Decision taken: discard the idea and keep the retained solver unchanged. This particular watch-list payload change adds enough Python-side decoding and bookkeeping to outweigh the saved watched-slot normalization work.
- Next recommended step: do not revisit watch-list payload changes whose only purpose is encoding the watched slot or avoiding `lits[0] == false_literal` normalization. The next worthwhile propagation idea still needs to reduce actual ternary relocation/unit work, not just reshuffle how the watch metadata is represented in Python.

The next real solver win still needs to come from watched-clause traversal or conflict analysis on the dense UNSAT bottlenecks, not from broadening the current phase-policy portfolio.

## 2026-03-20T08:18:14+01:00 - Kept reason-size bucket profiling for conflict analysis

- Objective: make the next `analyze()` / `minimize_learnt()` experiment evidence-driven by measuring which reason-clause sizes actually dominate traversal and successful minimization.
- Hypothesis: if the profiler records reason-size buckets for conflict-analysis traversals and for kept-vs-removed literals during minimization, it will tell us whether future conflict-analysis work should target tiny reasons, medium reasons, or avoid spending effort on large reasons that rarely help.
- Files changed: `tools/profile_solver.py`, `tests/test_profile_solver.py`, `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `maybe.md`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python tools/profile_solver.py large/test_6.cnf special/hard.cnf medium/test_4.cnf large/test_8.cnf`
- Benchmark results: the profiler now records reason-size buckets for `analyze()` traversals and `minimize_learnt()` kept/removed literals, using buckets `2..3`, `4..9`, and `10+`. On the hard UNSAT bottlenecks, conflict analysis is overwhelmingly driven by tiny reasons: `large/test_6.cnf` saw `1,057,846` reason traversals, with `931,316` (`88.04%`) in `2..3`, `78,664` (`7.44%`) in `4..9`, and only `47,866` (`4.53%`) in `10+`; `special/hard.cnf` and `medium/test_4.cnf` showed the same shape (`85.51%` and `88.16%` in `2..3`). Minimization removals are also dominated by tiny reasons but still get a real secondary contribution from `4..9`: on `large/test_6.cnf`, removed literals were `112,785` from `2..3`, `27,850` from `4..9`, and only `4,012` from `10+`; on `special/hard.cnf`, `86,242`, `22,901`, and `1,885`; on `medium/test_4.cnf`, `19,271`, `4,808`, and `272`. The SAT-like `large/test_8.cnf` is the contrast case: large reasons rarely help minimization there (`10+` dropped only `2,856` literals) but are often kept (`19,467`), which reinforces that heavy work on large reasons is especially risky on that family.
- Decision taken: keep the profiler/test upgrade and leave the production solver unchanged. The repo now has a much better target profile for future conflict-analysis work.
- Next recommended step: if conflict analysis is revisited, bias toward a fast path for `2..3`-literal reasons and possibly a selective `4..9` path, but be very skeptical of extra work on `10+` reasons, especially on SAT-like cases such as `large/test_8.cnf`.
