On the four main hotspot cases, batches containing problem ternary clauses were mixed `61.93%` on `large/test_6.cnf`, `70.78%` on `special/hard.cnf`, `57.85%` on `medium/test_4.cnf`, and `47.21%` on `large/test_8.cnf`. Average batch size was only `4.33..5.90`, but average non-problem co-resident watchers were still `0.96..2.36` per batch, and learnt-large clauses dominated the co-resident side at `17.68%..34.61%` of all watchers inside those batches. So separate physical watch lists are not obviously a dead end on batch-homogeneity grounds, even though earlier branch-hoisting and clause-local-state branches still lost.
- Decision taken: keep the profiler/test upgrade and leave the production solver unchanged. This is a measurement keep, not a solver-speed keep.
- Next recommended step: if propagation is revisited, distinguish carefully between ideas that only branch earlier inside the same mixed watcher list and ideas that truly separate problem-ternary and learnt-large traversal. The former is already a known dead end; the latter still has real mixed-list work behind it and may be worth a bounded scratch experiment if it preserves watch semantics without extra per-clause side state.

## 2026-03-20T23:18:27+01:00 - Kept reusable hotspot A/B comparison tooling

- Objective: eliminate the repeated ad hoc shell snippets used for forward/reverse hotspot A/B checks by adding a standard tool that compares two runners with validation and order-aware totals.
- Hypothesis: because many recent cycles live or die on small hotspot A/B signals rather than full-suite runs, a reusable comparison tool should reduce measurement friction and prevent avoidable mistakes like shell quoting/history-expansion issues or inconsistent validation between runs.
- Files changed: `tools/hotspot_compare.py`, `tests/test_hotspot_compare.py`, `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `benchmark_suite.py`, and `next_steps.md`; added `tools/hotspot_compare.py` with module-vs-module / module-vs-CLI / CLI-vs-CLI support, forward+reverse ordering, repeat-aware median representative times, and checker-backed validation via the existing benchmark helpers; added regression coverage in `tests/test_hotspot_compare.py`; `python -m py_compile tools/hotspot_compare.py tests/test_hotspot_compare.py`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python tools/hotspot_compare.py --baseline-module satsolver --candidate-cli-script satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf special/tseitin.cnf`.
- Benchmark results: the new tool works end-to-end and gives the exact kind of output the recent cycles have been reconstructing by hand. On the smoke comparison, module-mode `satsolver` versus exact-CLI `satsolver.py` over `small/test_1.cnf` and `special/tseitin.cnf`, the tool reported both forward and reverse totals, validated SAT and UNSAT outputs cleanly, and showed the expected CLI startup overhead (`0.0013s` vs `0.0761s` forward, `0.0010s` vs `0.0834s` reverse, two-order average `0.0011s` vs `0.0798s`).
- Decision taken: keep the tooling upgrade and leave the production solver unchanged. This is a benchmark-workflow keep, not a solver-speed keep.
- Next recommended step: use `tools/hotspot_compare.py` for future forward/reverse hotspot A/B checks before spending full-suite benchmark time on small solver changes.

## 2026-03-20T23:37:45+01:00 - Rejected physical problem-ternary watcher split

- Objective: test whether a true physical split between original problem-ternary watcher traffic and the remaining watcher families can remove enough mixed-list traversal work to beat the retained serial baseline.
- Hypothesis: because the latest profiler says batches containing problem ternary clauses are often mixed with learnt-large watchers, moving original non-learnt ternary clauses into dedicated watch lists might simplify the dominant propagation path more effectively than earlier branch-hoisting or clause-local-side-state experiments.
- Files changed: `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `next_steps.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, and the current `propagate()` hot path; built and removed temporary `scratch/exp_problem_ternary_watch_split.py`; `python -m py_compile scratch/exp_problem_ternary_watch_split.py`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python -u - <<'PY' ... baseline-vs-candidate serial compare on large/test_6.cnf, special/hard.cnf, large/test_8.cnf with conflict counts ... PY`.
- Benchmark results: the split changed the search path and lost badly on the mixed representative cases. On `large/test_6.cnf`, the retained serial baseline took `11.9614s` with `59,201` conflicts, while the split candidate took `16.9394s` with `81,161` conflicts. On `special/hard.cnf`, it did help (`7.9703s` / `44,619` -> `6.8413s` / `39,511`), but `large/test_8.cnf` blew up from `3.3173s` with `16,430` conflicts to `9.2863s` with `44,287` conflicts. An earlier full forward/reverse `tools/hotspot_compare.py` attempt also ran abnormally long enough to confirm this was already a losing branch before the narrower direct timing script finished. So this is another family-specific propagation trap rather than a stable structural win.
- Decision taken: discard the physical split-list scratch branch and leave the production solver unchanged.
- Next recommended step: if watch-list separation is ever revisited again, it needs a much stronger semantics-preservation story than simply moving original problem-ternary clauses into dedicated lists and traversing them separately. Mixed-batch statistics alone are not enough justification.

## 2026-03-20T23:37:45+01:00 - Kept `satsolver_fast.py` as a lean exact-CLI candidate

- Objective: try a materially different top-level solver file for the real submission path, not by changing the CDCL core again, but by removing the current root pure-literal presolve from an alternate wrapper and testing whether the exact-CLI path gets faster overall.
- Hypothesis: because the root-pure gate remains a real startup/search-path tradeoff and the historical best exact-CLI snapshot predates the current narrow root-pure keep, a lean wrapper around the current solver core with no root-pure presolve may beat the current same-day CLI path even if the dense hotspot slice is only near-tied.
- Files changed: `satsolver_fast.py`, `tests/test_solver_fast.py`, `out_fast_cli_extended.txt`, `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, and the current root-pure notes; prototyped a no-root-pure serial compare in-process on the five hotspot cases; added `satsolver_fast.py` as a lean wrapper around the current solver core without root-pure presolve but with the same presolvers and portfolio gate; `python -m py_compile satsolver_fast.py`; `python satsolver_fast.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_sat.txt`; `python satsolver_fast.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; exact-CLI A/B on the tiny smoke slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; exact-CLI A/B on the five hotspot cases via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; same-day full exact-CLI single runs of `satsolver.py` and `satsolver_fast.py` via `benchmark_suite.py`; repeat-aware exact-CLI full-suite run of `satsolver_fast.py` via `benchmark_suite.py --repeat 2`; added `tests/test_solver_fast.py`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`.
- Benchmark results: the lean wrapper is a real exact-CLI candidate. Tiny exact-CLI smoke improved from `0.0825s` to `0.0627s` on the two-order average. The exact-CLI five-case hotspot slice also stayed modestly positive at `24.1051s -> 24.0114s` average. On the same-day exact-CLI full 59-case single run, the current solver took `29.3963s`, while `satsolver_fast.py` solved `59/59` correctly in `28.4781s` (`-3.12%`) with a lower worst case on `large/test_6.cnf` (`12.8413s -> 12.4722s`). The repeat-aware exact-CLI confirmation for `satsolver_fast.py` landed at `27.3829s` representative in `out_fast_cli_extended.txt`, still `59/59` correct. That does not beat the historical best exact-CLI snapshot `27.1974s`, but it does beat the latest current exact-CLI reruns (`27.6136s` and `27.7868s`) and gives us a stronger current alternate submission candidate than the retained main file.
- Decision taken: keep `satsolver_fast.py` plus light regression coverage as an alternate exact-CLI solver candidate, and leave `satsolver.py` unchanged for now.
- Next recommended step: if we want to merge this win back into `satsolver.py`, the next experiment should be a smarter exact-CLI-oriented root-pure policy rather than a blind removal. `satsolver_fast.py` shows the current root-pure gate is still costly enough on the real submission path to justify further tuning, but the historical best exact-CLI snapshot means the evidence still is not strong enough to replace the main file outright.

## 2026-03-21T00:00:40+01:00 - Rejected removing the SAT self-check from `satsolver_fast.py`

- Objective: test whether the alternate exact-CLI wrapper can get another cheap submission-path win by skipping the post-solve `model_satisfies(...)` sanity check before writing SAT output.
- Hypothesis: because `satsolver_fast.py` is already a lean submission candidate, removing the internal SAT self-check might shave enough SAT-only CLI overhead to matter without touching the underlying CDCL search path.
- Files changed: `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: temporarily removed the `model_satisfies(...)` guard from `satsolver_fast.py` and then restored it after measurement; `python -m py_compile satsolver_fast.py`; `python satsolver_fast.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_sat.txt`; `python satsolver_fast.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python tools/hotspot_compare.py --baseline-cli-script satsolver.py --candidate-cli-script satsolver_fast.py small/test_1.cnf small/test_5.cnf small/test_6.cnf small/test_9.cnf medium/test_8.cnf medium/test_9.cnf large/test_1.cnf large/test_2.cnf large/test_5.cnf large/test_7.cnf large/test_9.cnf special/easy.cnf`; `python tools/hotspot_compare.py --baseline-cli-script satsolver.py --candidate-cli-script satsolver_fast.py small/test_1.cnf small/test_10.cnf medium/test_3.cnf medium/test_4.cnf large/test_6.cnf large/test_8.cnf large/test_10.cnf special/hard.cnf special/tseitin.cnf`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver_fast /tmp/fast_cli_suite_repeat2_20260320_nomodelcheck.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16 --cli-script satsolver_fast.py --repeat 2`; `python - <<'PY' ... time cumulative model_satisfies(...) cost on all SAT benchmark cases ... PY`.
- Benchmark results: the removed work was far smaller than it looked. The SAT-heavy root-hit exact-CLI slice versus current `satsolver.py` stayed clearly positive at `0.5238s -> 0.4232s` two-order average, and the broader mixed exact-CLI slice versus current also stayed positive at `24.4615s -> 24.2157s`. But direct measurement showed the removed self-check only costs `0.005311s` total across all `28` SAT benchmark cases, with the worst single case only `0.000713s` on `large/test_8.cnf`. The repeat-aware full exact-CLI rerun of the no-self-check variant landed at `27.6785s`, which is worse than the retained `satsolver_fast.py` snapshot `27.3829s` and still above the historical exact-CLI best `27.1974s`. So this was not a meaningful real submission-path lever.
- Decision taken: discard the no-self-check variant and restore the internal SAT self-check in `satsolver_fast.py`.
- Next recommended step: keep `satsolver_fast.py` lean, but do not spend more time on tiny CLI-only safety removals unless their removed work is at least one or two orders of magnitude larger than this. The next worthwhile exact-CLI move is still a smarter root-pure policy or a deeper solver-core win, not shaving millisecond-scale postchecks.

## 2026-03-21T00:10:14+01:00 - Rejected parse-time first-round-pure gating in `satsolver_fast.py`

- Objective: test whether `satsolver_fast.py` can recover some of the historical exact-CLI win from root pure-literal preprocessing without paying the full iterative root-pure scan on almost every CLI run.
- Hypothesis: because every current root-pure hit case already has at least two first-round pure literals, collecting first-round polarity counts during DIMACS parsing might let the alternate wrapper run the full iterative root-pure presolve only on likely-hit formulas, preserving any benefit on those cases while keeping the no-root-pure startup win elsewhere.
- Files changed: `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `next_steps.md`, and the current `satsolver_fast.py`; confirmed on the full benchmark corpus that all current `len(root_pure_literals) >= 2` cases also have `first_round_pure_count >= 2`; backed up the retained `satsolver_fast.py`; temporarily added parse-time first-round polarity counting plus a gated call to `find_iterative_root_pure_literals(...)` inside `satsolver_fast.py`; `python -m py_compile satsolver_fast.py`; `python satsolver_fast.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_sat.txt`; `python satsolver_fast.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; exact-CLI A/B of old-vs-new `satsolver_fast.py` on the 12 current root-hit SAT cases via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; exact-CLI A/B of old-vs-new `satsolver_fast.py` on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; restored the retained `satsolver_fast.py`; `python -m py_compile satsolver_fast.py`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`.
- Benchmark results: the idea did not earn a full-suite run. On the exact-CLI root-hit slice, the old wrapper beat the parse-gated variant by a hair on the two-order average (`0.4382s -> 0.4407s` candidate, worse), with mixed per-case wins and losses. On the mixed exact-CLI slice, the parse-gated variant was only effectively tied-to-slightly-positive (`24.6599s -> 24.6480s`), and that tiny edge came from noisy case-level movement rather than a clear systematic win. In other words, even after collecting first-round pure counts during parsing, bringing root-pure preprocessing back into the alternate wrapper still did not produce a strong enough exact-CLI signal to justify a full-suite benchmark.
- Decision taken: discard the parse-time first-round-pure gate and restore the retained no-root-pure `satsolver_fast.py`.
- Next recommended step: keep treating `satsolver_fast.py` as the lean alternate exact-CLI baseline. If exact-submission work revisits root-pure policy again, it needs a materially stronger classifier or a broader win than this near-tie parser-gate branch.

## 2026-03-21T00:15:44+01:00 - Rejected standalone `satsolver_fast.py` copy of the main solver

- Objective: test whether the alternate exact-CLI candidate can get faster by becoming a standalone no-root-pure copy of `satsolver.py` instead of a thin wrapper that imports the main solver module.
- Hypothesis: because `satsolver_fast.py` currently imports the entire main solver, replacing that wrapper with a direct standalone copy of the current solver core minus root-pure presolve might reduce exact-CLI startup/import overhead enough to improve the submission path.

- Tests executed: backed up the retained wrapper as `scratch_satsolver_fast_before_standalone.py`; copied `satsolver.py` into `satsolver_fast.py`; removed root-pure presolve from `solve_cnf_serial()` and adjusted the CLI usage line; `python -m py_compile satsolver_fast.py scratch_satsolver_fast_before_standalone.py`; `python satsolver_fast.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_sat.txt`; `python satsolver_fast.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; exact-CLI A/B of wrapper-vs-standalone on the 12 root-hit SAT cases via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; exact-CLI A/B of wrapper-vs-standalone on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; restored the retained wrapper; `python -m py_compile satsolver_fast.py`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`.

On the mixed exact-CLI hotspot slice it was only slightly positive overall (`24.4869s -> 24.4441s`), with the win concentrated on a few dense UNSAT cases and the loss concentrated on the lighter SAT and easy-UNSAT cases. That means the larger standalone script's own parse/import cost is outweighing any saved wrapper indirection on the submission path, especially on the easy formulas.
- Decision taken: discard the standalone-copy branch and restore the retained wrapper-style `satsolver_fast.py`.
- Next recommended step: keep `satsolver_fast.py` as a thin alternate wrapper rather than replacing it with a copied standalone solver file. If we revisit alternate exact-CLI files again, they need to be materially smaller or structurally different than the current main solver, not just a duplicated no-root-pure copy.

So reshaping the wrapper alone is not enough to beat the retained alternate baseline.

- Benchmark results: this branch failed decisively before any full-suite run. The exact-CLI SAT-heavy root-hit slice regressed on the two-order average (`0.4081s -> 0.4182s`). The mixed nine-case hotspot slice blew up from `24.3616s` to `32.2099s`, and the reason was obvious: `special/tseitin.cnf` jumped from `0.0298s` to `7.4602s` forward and from `0.0341s` to `7.4312s` reverse because the scratch alternate no longer had the structural XOR presolver. Other hotspot cases stayed near baseline, but that single missing structural win was enough to make the whole idea non-viable.
- Decision taken: discard the lazy-core alternate and keep the retained `satsolver_fast.py` unchanged.
- Next recommended step: future alternate exact-CLI files should treat structural pigeonhole/XOR presolvers as required semantics-preserving shortcuts, not optional startup overhead. Their aggregate full-corpus cost may be small, but removing them makes specific instances catastrophically worse, so future alternate-file work should keep them and look elsewhere for exact-CLI gains.

## 2026-03-21T00:58:40+01:00 - Kept byte-level DIMACS parsing in `satsolver_fast.py`

- Objective: reduce exact-CLI overhead on the alternate submission-path solver by replacing the text DIMACS read path in `satsolver_fast.py` with a byte-oriented parser and a local byte-oriented output writer, without changing the underlying CDCL behavior or structural presolvers.
- Hypothesis: exact-CLI timing still pays parser/read overhead on every case, especially the lighter SAT and easy-UNSAT instances, so a smaller but semantically identical byte-level parse/write path could produce a real same-day submission-path win even after several alternate-wrapper reshapes failed.
- Files changed: `satsolver_fast.py`, `tests/test_solver_fast.py`, `out_fast_cli_extended.txt`, `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `satsolver_fast.py`, `benchmark_summary.md`, and `next_steps.md`; created scratch candidate `scratch_satsolver_fast_bytesparse.py` with local byte-level `parse_dimacs_file()` plus local `write_result()`; `python -m py_compile scratch_satsolver_fast_bytesparse.py`; `python scratch_satsolver_fast_bytesparse.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/scratch_fast_bytesparse_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/scratch_fast_bytesparse_sat.txt`; `python scratch_satsolver_fast_bytesparse.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/scratch_fast_bytesparse_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/scratch_fast_bytesparse_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; exact-CLI A/B of retained `satsolver_fast.py` vs scratch candidate on the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py --repeat 2`; exact-CLI A/B on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; same-day repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case suites of retained `satsolver_fast.py` and the scratch candidate via `benchmark_suite.py --repeat 2`; merged the byte parser and byte writer into `satsolver_fast.py`; added parser regressions in `tests/test_solver_fast.py`; deleted `scratch_satsolver_fast_bytesparse.py`; `python -m py_compile satsolver_fast.py tests/test_solver_fast.py`; `python satsolver_fast.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_sat.txt`; `python satsolver_fast.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/satsolver_fast_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver_fast out_fast_cli_extended.txt small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more --bruteforce-var-limit 16 --cli-script satsolver_fast.py --repeat 2`.
- Benchmark results: the parser branch was modest but real on the right path. On the exact-CLI SAT-heavy root-hit slice, the two-order average improved from `0.4431s` to `0.4319s`. On the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice, it was effectively tied overall (`24.6944s -> 24.6945s`), with small wins on `medium/test_4.cnf`, `large/test_10.cnf`, and `special/hard.cnf` offset by small losses on `large/test_6.cnf`, `large/test_8.cnf`, and `special/tseitin.cnf`. The real gate was the same-day repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case comparison: retained `satsolver_fast.py` landed at `27.6459s` representative (`55.2917s` measured), while the scratch byte-parser candidate improved that to `27.4523s` (`54.9045s` measured), both `59/59` correct. After merging, the refreshed repo artifact in `out_fast_cli_extended.txt` landed at `27.5074s` representative (`55.0148s` measured), still `59/59` correct. That is better than the same-day retained baseline but still above the historical best retained `satsolver_fast.py` snapshot `27.3829s`, so this is a same-day alternate-path improvement rather than a new all-time exact-CLI record.
- Decision taken: keep the byte-level `parse_dimacs[_file]()` and local byte-output path in `satsolver_fast.py`, along with new parser regressions in `tests/test_solver_fast.py`.
- Next recommended step: treat parser/read/write overhead as a real but small exact-CLI lever. Future alternate-file work can revisit startup parsing, but it should keep the structural pigeonhole/XOR presolvers intact and require same-day repeat-aware baseline-vs-candidate comparisons before keeping sub-1% exact-CLI changes.

## 2026-03-21T01:08:55+01:00 - Rejected byte-level parse/write path on main `satsolver.py`

## 2026-03-21T01:27:12+01:00 - Rejected single-worker phase-bias replacement for the narrow portfolio gate in `satsolver_fast.py`

- Objective: test whether the retained alternate exact-CLI solver can beat its current narrow two-worker portfolio by replacing that gate with a single serial phase-biased solve, avoiding the `multiprocessing` path entirely on the one family where the gate currently fires.

But on the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice, the candidate lost overall on the two-order average (`24.3702s -> 24.5513s`). The candidate did help `large/test_8.cnf` and one forward `large/test_6.cnf` run, but it lost enough startup-sensitive cases plus the reverse heavy-case ordering that the overall signal stayed negative.

## 2026-03-21T01:33:25+01:00 - Rejected more aggressive learnt-clause database trimming in `satsolver.py`

- Objective: test whether the main solver can cut the remaining learnt-large propagation cost by deleting a larger share of ranked reduction candidates inside `reduce_database()`.
- Hypothesis: the profiler still shows learnt-large relocation as a big secondary watcher-churn bucket, so keeping fewer ranked candidates during database reduction might shrink the live learnt-large set enough to help the hard UNSAT cases without breaking correctness.
- Files changed: `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`, `satsolver.py`, and `tools/profile_solver.py`; materialized the retained main solver into `_tmp_satsolver_baseline_reduce_aggr.py`; temporarily patched `satsolver.py` so `reduce_database()` kept only the top `40%` of ranked candidates instead of the top `50%`; `python -m py_compile satsolver.py`; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/reduce_aggr_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/reduce_aggr_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/reduce_aggr_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/reduce_aggr_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; exact-CLI A/B of baseline-vs-candidate on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; reverted `satsolver.py`; deleted `_tmp_satsolver_baseline_reduce_aggr.py`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`.
- Benchmark results: the branch failed decisively and changed the search path in a bad way. On the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice, the two-order average regressed from `24.6309s` to `40.4076s`. The main failure was SAT-like `large/test_8.cnf`, which blew up from `0.2769s -> 12.1894s` forward and `0.3061s -> 12.1799s` reverse while still validating SAT. The dense UNSAT cases also regressed: `large/test_6.cnf` `11.8966s -> 13.9341s` forward and `12.2028s -> 13.7970s` reverse, `special/hard.cnf` `8.4888s -> 9.8272s` forward and `7.7342s -> 9.2954s` reverse, `medium/test_4.cnf` `1.7207s -> 1.9746s` forward and `1.6967s -> 2.0813s` reverse, and `large/test_10.cnf` `1.6534s -> 2.0260s` forward and `1.6537s -> 1.9606s` reverse. So simply trimming the ranked candidate pool harder is not a viable learnt-large fix.
- Decision taken: discard the more aggressive database-trimming branch and keep the retained `satsolver.py` unchanged.
- Next recommended step: treat broad reduction aggressiveness as another learnt-retention dead end unless a future idea uses a better classifier than “same ranking, keep a smaller fraction.” This branch says that shrinking the live learnt set harder can catastrophically damage SAT-like search even when the original motivation comes from learnt-large watcher churn.

On the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice, the two-order average regressed from `24.4791s` to `30.9079s`. Forward order regressed from `24.2045s` to `30.8099s`, and reverse order regressed from `24.7536s` to `31.0059s`. The biggest blow-up was SAT-like `large/test_8.cnf`, which jumped from `0.2864s -> 2.3767s` forward and `0.2913s -> 2.2581s` reverse while still validating SAT. The dense UNSAT cases also regressed cleanly: `large/test_6.cnf` `11.9492s -> 13.2572s` forward and `12.1819s -> 13.1447s` reverse, `special/hard.cnf` `7.7986s -> 10.0826s` forward and `8.2355s -> 10.5278s` reverse, and `large/test_10.cnf` `1.6020s -> 2.3006s` forward and `1.6012s -> 2.2972s` reverse.
- Decision taken: discard the earlier-reduction scheduling branch and keep the retained `satsolver.py` unchanged.
- Next recommended step: treat “same ranking, same keep fraction, reduce sooner” as another learnt-database dead end unless a future idea uses a materially better classifier than a smaller `next_reduce` growth factor. This branch says that simply revisiting the reduction cadence can damage both dense UNSAT and SAT-like search at once.

## 2026-03-21T01:51:02+01:00 - Rejected shorter-first learnt-clause ranking in `reduce_database()`

- Objective: test whether the main solver can shrink learnt-large watcher churn more safely by changing only the reduction ranking, keeping the same top-half retention policy and reduction schedule.

- Benchmark results: the ranking-only branch failed hard enough that it did not earn a full-suite run. On the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice, the two-order average regressed from `24.9575s` to `30.6752s`. Forward order regressed from `25.4716s` to `30.3040s`, and reverse order regressed from `24.4434s` to `31.0463s`. The worst losses were `large/test_6.cnf` (`12.9431s -> 16.5156s` forward, `12.0496s -> 17.2031s` reverse) and `large/test_8.cnf` (`0.2815s -> 0.9161s` forward, `0.2814s -> 0.9101s` reverse). `special/hard.cnf` and `large/test_10.cnf` also got worse in both orders, while only `medium/test_3.cnf` and `special/tseitin.cnf` showed small wins.
- Decision taken: discard the shorter-first ranking branch and keep the retained `satsolver.py` unchanged.
- Next recommended step: treat “same keep fraction, but prefer shorter learnt clauses before activity within the same LBD” as another learnt-database dead end. Future learnt-large work still needs a materially better classifier than reshuffling length versus activity inside the existing reduction sort.

## 2026-03-21T02:06:22+01:00 - Rejected a narrow `satsolver_fast.py` / `satsolver.py` hybrid exact-CLI wrapper

Forward order lost `24.4419s -> 24.6735s`, and reverse order lost `24.8899s -> 25.3875s`. At the same time, `special/hard.cnf` regressed in reverse (`8.3161s -> 9.0484s`), `large/test_10.cnf` regressed in both orders, and the startup-sensitive small cases were noisy enough that the hybrid lost more there than it gained from the single reverse `large/test_6.cnf` win.
- Decision taken: discard the narrow hybrid wrapper and keep the retained solver files unchanged.

## 2026-03-21T02:14:10+01:00 - Rejected removing learnt-clause activity bumping in `analyze()`

Even the mid-size UNSAT cases regressed, so this was not just one family going noisy.

## 2026-03-21T02:22:29+01:00 - Rejected activity-per-literal learnt ranking in `reduce_database()`

- Objective: test whether the main solver can shrink learnt-large watcher churn more safely by making the reduction classifier size-aware without repeating the blunt shorter-first tie-break failure.
- Hypothesis: because learnt-clause activity is clearly essential but large learnt clauses still drive much of the secondary watcher churn, ranking reduction candidates by activity per literal within each LBD bucket might preserve impactful short clauses while pushing less efficient long clauses out of the kept half more often.
- Files changed: `agent_log.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `next_steps.md`, and `satsolver.py`; copied `satsolver.py` to `scratch_satsolver_reduce_act_per_len.py`; materialized the retained baseline to `_tmp_satsolver_baseline_reduce_act_per_len.py`; patched the scratch solver so `reduce_database()` sorted candidates by `(lbd, -(activity / len(lits)), len(lits), -activity)` instead of `(lbd, -activity, len(lits))`; `python -m py_compile scratch_satsolver_reduce_act_per_len.py`; `python scratch_satsolver_reduce_act_per_len.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/reduce_act_per_len_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/reduce_act_per_len_sat.txt`; `python scratch_satsolver_reduce_act_per_len.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/reduce_act_per_len_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/reduce_act_per_len_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; exact-CLI A/B of baseline-vs-candidate on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; deleted the scratch candidate and temporary baseline after the branch regressed.
- Benchmark results: the classifier tweak failed clearly enough that it did not earn a full-suite run. On the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice, the two-order average regressed from `25.3946s` to `30.0746s`. Forward order regressed from `24.9354s` to `29.9795s`, and reverse order regressed from `25.8538s` to `30.1696s`. The main damage was `large/test_6.cnf` (`12.1182s -> 16.5862s` forward, `13.3722s -> 16.8649s` reverse) and `large/test_8.cnf` (`0.2813s -> 1.3741s` forward, `0.2895s -> 1.3303s` reverse), even though `special/hard.cnf`, `medium/test_3.cnf`, and `medium/test_4.cnf` improved modestly. So size-normalized activity is still not a strong enough classifier to keep SAT-like search stable.
- Decision taken: discard the activity-per-literal ranking branch and keep the retained `satsolver.py` unchanged.

## 2026-03-21T02:44:19+01:00 - Promoted the faster wrapper path into `satsolver.py` via `satsolver_core.py`

- Objective: turn the retained exact-CLI alternate into the main submission path if same-day evidence showed it was faster than the current `satsolver.py` strongly enough to justify a real promotion.
- Hypothesis: the current exact-CLI alternate already had the best same-day submission-path signal in the repo, so splitting the old full solver into a helper core module and making `satsolver.py` use the byte-parser/no-root-pure wrapper path should speed up the required `python satsolver.py input.cnf output.txt` command while preserving the internal API that tests and tools still use.
- Files changed: `agent_log.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `next_steps.md`, `out_extended.txt`, `out_cli_extended.txt`, `satsolver.py`, `satsolver_core.py`, `satsolver_fast.py`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `agent_log.md`, `next_steps.md`, and `benchmark_summary.md`; exact-CLI A/B of retained `satsolver.py` vs retained `satsolver_fast.py` on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; exact-CLI A/B on the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py --repeat 2`; repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case suites of retained `satsolver.py` and retained `satsolver_fast.py` via `benchmark_suite.py --cli-script ... --repeat 2`; copied the old `satsolver.py` to `satsolver_core.py`; rewrote `satsolver.py` as the faster exact-CLI wrapper while keeping `solve_cnf_serial()` plus the core symbols available for tests/tools; pointed `satsolver_fast.py` at `satsolver_core.py`; `python -m py_compile satsolver.py satsolver_core.py satsolver_fast.py`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; `python satsolver.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/main_wrapper_sat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py small/test_1.cnf /tmp/main_wrapper_sat.txt`; `python satsolver.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/main_wrapper_unsat.txt`; `python tools/checker.py special/tseitin.cnf /tmp/main_wrapper_unsat.txt --bruteforce-var-limit 0`; exact-CLI A/B of `HEAD:satsolver.py` vs promoted `satsolver.py` on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; exact-CLI A/B on the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py --repeat 2`; exact-CLI A/B of promoted `satsolver.py` vs retained `satsolver_fast.py` on both slices; refreshed `out_extended.txt` with `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_extended.txt ... --repeat 2`; refreshed `out_cli_extended.txt` with `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_cli_extended.txt ... --cli-script satsolver.py --repeat 2`.
- Benchmark results: the promotion cleared the same-day exact-CLI gate against the previous mainline. Before the code move, the retained `satsolver_fast.py` beat the old `satsolver.py` on the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice (`26.0630s -> 25.2227s` two-order average), on the SAT-heavy root-hit slice (`0.6018s -> 0.4941s`), and on the repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case suite (`30.3078s -> 29.1527s`, both `59/59` correct). After promotion, the new `satsolver.py` beat the old `HEAD` mainline on the root-hit slice (`0.5026s -> 0.4044s` two-order average), on the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice (`24.5923s -> 24.1644s`), and on the repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case suite (`30.3078s -> 29.5746s`, `59/59` correct). The promoted `satsolver.py` also stayed competitive with sibling `satsolver_fast.py` on the same-day slice checks: root-hit `0.4516s -> 0.4391s` and mixed hotspot `25.2656s -> 24.5747s` in favor of the new main file, even though the separate `satsolver_fast.py` full repeat-aware rerun was still a little faster on one broad suite run (`29.1527s` vs `29.5746s`). The refreshed in-process repeat-aware 59-case artifact for the promoted main file landed at `27.6294s`, which is not a new historical best there.
- Decision taken: keep the helper-module split and the promoted wrapper `satsolver.py` because it materially improved the required same-day exact-CLI submission path relative to the previous mainline while staying correct and test-clean.
- Next recommended step: benchmark future exact-CLI work against the promoted wrapper `satsolver.py`, not the old monolithic main file. The next good exact-CLI keep should either beat the promoted wrapper on both same-day hotspot A/B and same-day repeat-aware full-suite A/B, or close the remaining gap to the best historical exact-CLI snapshot without sacrificing the exported `Solver` / `solve_cnf_serial` compatibility that the repo tooling depends on.

## 2026-03-21T02:57:52+01:00 - Kept runtime `typing` import cleanup in the wrapper/core path

- Objective: test whether the promoted main submission path can get a small exact-CLI startup win by removing runtime `typing` imports that are no longer needed under `from __future__ import annotations`.
- Hypothesis: because `satsolver.py`, `satsolver_fast.py`, and `satsolver_core.py` only use postponed annotations, replacing `typing.Optional` with `| None` and moving the core's iterable hint to the lighter `collections.abc.Iterable` should trim script startup cost enough to matter on exact-CLI runs, especially the SAT-heavy root-hit slice, without changing solver semantics.
- Files changed: `agent_log.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `next_steps.md`, `out_cli_extended.txt`, `satsolver.py`, `satsolver_fast.py`, `satsolver_core.py`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `next_steps.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, and `agent_log.md`; `python -X importtime -c "import satsolver"` and `python -X importtime -c "import satsolver_core"` on the retained baseline to confirm `typing` showed up materially on import; patched `satsolver.py`, `satsolver_fast.py`, and `satsolver_core.py` to remove runtime `typing` imports, use `| None` annotations, and import `Iterable` from `collections.abc`; `python -m py_compile satsolver.py satsolver_fast.py satsolver_core.py`; refreshed import-time smoke checks after the patch; materialized the retained `HEAD` baseline into `/tmp/satsolver_head_typing_baseline/{satsolver.py,satsolver_core.py}`; exact-CLI A/B of baseline-vs-candidate on the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py --repeat 2`; exact-CLI A/B on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; repeat-aware exact-CLI full-suite baseline run on `small medium large special satlib_subset satlib_more` via `benchmark_suite.py --cli-script /tmp/satsolver_head_typing_baseline/satsolver.py --repeat 2`; repeat-aware exact-CLI candidate run on the same 59-case corpus via `benchmark_suite.py --cli-script satsolver.py --repeat 2`; refreshed `out_cli_extended.txt` from the kept 59-case candidate artifact; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`.
- Benchmark results: the startup-sensitive and full-suite exact-CLI gates all stayed positive. On the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice, the two-order average improved from `0.4338s` to `0.3878s`. On the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice, the two-order average improved from `24.5782s` to `24.4033s`, with forward `24.4462s -> 23.8638s` and reverse `24.7103s -> 24.9429s` still netting a small overall win. Most importantly, the proper same-day repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case suite improved from `28.3088s` on the frozen `HEAD` baseline to `27.9510s` on the candidate, still `59/59` correct. This is a real keep against the current mainline, but not a new historical best against the older `27.1974s` exact-CLI snapshot.
- Decision taken: keep the runtime-import cleanup in `satsolver.py`, `satsolver_fast.py`, and `satsolver_core.py` because it produced a small but positive same-day exact-CLI win on both the startup-sensitive slice and the repeat-aware 59-case suite without changing solver behavior.
- Next recommended step: treat runtime-only import cleanup as a valid but narrow exact-CLI lever. Future startup-path work should target similarly heavy imports or wrapper-only overhead, but it must still clear same-day repeat-aware 59-case confirmation because slice-only wins here can overstate the real gain.

- Next recommended step: treat "drop the wrapper's unused exported aliases" as another startup-path dead end unless a future alternate-file idea changes substantially more than alias surface area. ## 2026-03-21T03:23:52+01:00 - Rejected tokenized locked-clause marks in `reduce_database()`

- Objective: test whether the solver core can get a small exact-CLI win by removing the per-reduction Python `set` allocation for locked learnt clauses and replacing it with reusable tokenized clause marks.
- Hypothesis: because `reduce_database()` still runs on the hot conflict path and currently rebuilds a `set` from `self.reason` on every real reduction, replacing that with reusable integer marks on clause IDs might preserve the exact search path while shaving enough bookkeeping to matter on the mixed exact-CLI hotspot slice.
- Files changed: `agent_log.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `next_steps.md`

- Benchmark results: the candidate did not clear the first real gate, so it did not earn a full-suite run. On the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice, the two-order average regressed from `24.9111s` to `25.8251s`. The forward order was mildly positive (`25.1938s -> 24.9983s`), but the reverse order regressed hard (`24.6283s -> 26.6520s`), with the main damage on `large/test_6.cnf` (`12.1653s -> 14.3797s` reverse) and smaller regressions on `large/test_10.cnf`, `large/test_8.cnf`, `medium/test_3.cnf`, `small/test_10.cnf`, and `small/test_1.cnf`. The candidate stayed checker-valid on SAT and UNSAT smoke cases, but the mixed-slice reversal was too large to justify a broader run.
- Decision taken: discard the tokenized locked-mark branch and keep the retained solver files unchanged.
- Next recommended step: treat `reduce_database()` locked-clause allocation cleanup as another same-search-looking micro-optimization that still needs a stronger end-to-end signal than one positive order. Future learnt-database bookkeeping work should not assume that replacing small `set` allocations with reusable mark arrays is automatically a win on the current exact-CLI path.

## 2026-03-21T03:29:36+01:00 - Rejected parse-time structural summary collection in an alternate exact-CLI wrapper

- Objective: test whether a new alternate exact-CLI file can beat the retained `satsolver_fast.py` path by collecting structural presolve summaries during DIMACS parsing and then reusing them instead of rescanning clauses for pigeonhole, XOR, and portfolio gating.
- Hypothesis: because `satsolver_fast.py` currently does three front-loaded clause-list scans after parsing (`has_pigeonhole_core`, `xor_system_unsat`, and `should_use_parallel_portfolio`), a scratch wrapper that gathers those summaries incrementally while parsing might reduce submission-path startup enough to matter on the SAT-heavy exact-CLI slice without changing the solve path itself.

- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `next_steps.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `satsolver_fast.py`, and `satsolver_core.py`; copied `satsolver_fast.py` to `scratch_satsolver_statsfast.py`; patched the scratch file to add `FormulaStats`, parse-time structural summary collection, summary-based pigeonhole/XOR checks, and summary-based portfolio gating while keeping the retained solve path; `python -m py_compile scratch_satsolver_statsfast.py`; SAT/UNSAT smoke checks plus checker validation on `small/test_1.cnf` and `special/tseitin.cnf`; import-time smoke check with `python -X importtime -c "import scratch_satsolver_statsfast"`; exact-CLI A/B of retained `satsolver_fast.py` vs the scratch candidate on the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py --repeat 2`; deleted the scratch file after the candidate lost the first gate.

Forward order regressed from `0.3660s` to `0.4120s`, and reverse order regressed from `0.3977s` to `0.4415s`. The main losses were on startup-sensitive SAT cases like `large/test_5.cnf` (`0.0257s -> 0.0491s` forward), `large/test_7.cnf` (`0.0338s -> 0.0486s` forward), `large/test_1.cnf` (`0.0305s -> 0.0456s` forward), `small/test_6.cnf` (`0.0210s -> 0.0358s` reverse), and `small/test_1.cnf` (`0.0215s -> 0.0272s` reverse). The candidate stayed checker-valid on SAT and UNSAT smoke cases and had a competitive import-time smoke check (`scratch_satsolver_statsfast 2644us`), but merging the presolve work into parsing still lost overall.
- Decision taken: discard the parse-time structural-summary wrapper and keep the retained solver files unchanged.
- Next recommended step: treat “merge structural presolve scans into parsing” as another exact-CLI startup dead end unless a future design can show a materially smaller per-clause cost than both the current parser and the current standalone structural checks. Future alternate exact-CLI work should not assume that eliminating top-level rescans is automatically a win if the parser itself becomes heavier.

## 2026-03-21T03:37:34+01:00 - Rejected lazy export forwarding in `satsolver.py`

- Objective: test whether the promoted main wrapper can get a small exact-CLI startup win by replacing its broad top-level alias block with lazy module-level forwarding while preserving the test/tooling surface.
- Hypothesis: because the current `satsolver.py` still binds many aliases that exist mostly for tests and tooling, moving those exports behind module `__getattr__` and only resolving them when actually touched might reduce startup work on the exact submission path, while `globals().get(..., base....)` fallbacks would keep the patchable behavior that the regression tests rely on.
- Files changed: `agent_log.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `next_steps.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `satsolver.py`, and test/tooling references to `satsolver.*`; temporarily patched `satsolver.py` to replace the top-level alias block with `_FORWARDED_EXPORTS` plus module `__getattr__`, switched the exact-CLI path to `base.*` where appropriate, and used `globals().get(..., base....)` in `solve_cnf_serial()` / `solve_cnf_fast_serial()` so `mock.patch("satsolver.Solver", ...)` and `mock.patch("satsolver.find_iterative_root_pure_literals", ...)` would still work; `python -m py_compile satsolver.py satsolver_core.py satsolver_fast.py`; SAT/UNSAT smoke checks plus checker validation on `small/test_1.cnf` and `special/tseitin.cnf`; import-time smoke check with `python -X importtime -c "import satsolver"`; materialized the retained `HEAD` baseline into `/tmp/satsolver_lazyexports_baseline/{satsolver.py,satsolver_core.py}`; exact-CLI A/B of baseline-vs-candidate on the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py --repeat 2`; exact-CLI A/B on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; reverted `satsolver.py` after the mixed slice regressed.
- Benchmark results: the branch barely cleared the startup-sensitive root-hit gate and then failed the real mixed solver gate. On the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice, the two-order average improved slightly from `0.3592s` to `0.3524s`, losing forward (`0.3659s -> 0.3871s`) but winning reverse (`0.3526s -> 0.3176s`). But on the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice, the two-order average regressed from `24.1326s` to `24.4102s`. Forward order was nearly tied but still worse (`24.1847s -> 24.2247s`), and reverse order regressed more clearly (`24.0805s -> 24.5957s`), with the main damage on `special/hard.cnf` (`7.7451s -> 8.2155s` reverse) plus smaller losses on `large/test_6.cnf`, `large/test_8.cnf`, and `large/test_10.cnf`. The import-time smoke check also moved the wrong way overall (`satsolver 3017us`), so the slight root-hit win was not a broad startup improvement anyway.
- Decision taken: discard the lazy-export wrapper branch and keep the retained solver files unchanged.
- Next recommended step: treat lazy export forwarding as another startup-path dead end unless a future wrapper design removes materially more end-to-end work than top-level alias binding. Future `satsolver.py` startup work should not assume that preserving the same API behind `__getattr__` is enough to produce a net exact-CLI win.

## 2026-03-21T03:56:43+01:00 - Kept cached ternary clause flags in the shared solver core

- Objective: test whether the shared propagation hot path can be sped up by caching immutable ternary-vs-non-ternary clause shape on each `Clause`, so the watched-clause loop stops calling `len(lits) == 3` on every visit.
- Hypothesis: because `propagate()` is still the main retained hotspot and `len()` is still a visible Python-side cost there, replacing the repeated ternary shape check with a cached immutable `clause.ternary` flag might cut enough hot-loop overhead to matter without changing traversal order or search semantics.
- Files changed: `satsolver_core.py`, `tests/test_solver_regressions.py`, `out_cli_extended.txt`, `agent_log.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `next_steps.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `satsolver.py`, and `satsolver_core.py`; refreshed `cProfile` on `large/test_6.cnf`; confirmed the current main wrapper still beat `satsolver_fast.py` on the same-day root-hit and mixed exact-CLI slices; created scratch candidates `scratch_satsolver_core_ternaryflag.py` / `scratch_satsolver_ternaryflag.py`; patched the scratch core to add `Clause.ternary` and use it in `propagate()`; `python -m py_compile scratch_satsolver_ternaryflag.py scratch_satsolver_core_ternaryflag.py`; SAT/UNSAT smoke checks plus checker validation on `small/test_1.cnf` and `special/tseitin.cnf`; exact-CLI A/B of retained `satsolver.py` vs the scratch candidate on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; exact-CLI A/B on the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py --repeat 2`; same-day repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case baseline-vs-candidate runs via `benchmark_suite.py` to `/tmp/ternaryflag_{baseline,candidate}_cli_repeat2.txt`; merged the change into `satsolver_core.py`; added a regression for `Clause.ternary`; deleted the scratch files; `python -m py_compile satsolver.py satsolver_core.py satsolver_fast.py tests/test_solver_regressions.py`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; SAT/UNSAT checker smoke checks on the retained main wrapper; refreshed `out_cli_extended.txt` with `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_cli_extended.txt ... --cli-script satsolver.py --repeat 2`.
- Benchmark results: the scratch candidate cleared the real exact-CLI gates. On the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice, the two-order average improved from `25.9133s` to `25.0078s`, winning in both orders (`25.5562s -> 25.4490s` forward, `26.2705s -> 24.5666s` reverse). The SAT-heavy root-hit slice was effectively flat-to-slightly worse on the two-order average (`0.3694s -> 0.3722s`), so this is not a startup-path keep; it is a solver-core hot-loop keep. The same-day repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case baseline-vs-candidate comparison improved from `29.1167s` to `28.3019s`, still `59/59` correct. After merging into the tracked solver, the refreshed retained artifact in `out_cli_extended.txt` landed at `27.8701s`, also `59/59` correct.
- Decision taken: keep the cached ternary-flag change in `satsolver_core.py`, keep the new regression in `tests/test_solver_regressions.py`, and update the exact-CLI benchmark artifact.
- Next recommended step: treat small immutable clause-shape metadata as a still-live propagation lane, but keep the boundary tight. This keep does not rescue the earlier `Clause.size` or broader per-clause-side-state ideas; it only says that replacing one extremely frequent ternary shape query with an immutable cached bit can pay off. Future propagation work should keep looking for similarly cheap immutable hot-path metadata or broader relocation/unit simplifications, not mutable watch-side arrays or full duplicated clause metadata.

## 2026-03-21T04:08:44+01:00 - Rejected ternary-reason metadata reuse inside `minimize_learnt()`

- Objective: test whether the new immutable `Clause.ternary` flag can produce another solver-core win by removing `len(reason_lits)` checks from the hot one-hop redundancy path in `minimize_learnt()`.
- Hypothesis: because ternary reasons still dominate conflict analysis on the hard UNSAT cases and the just-kept `Clause.ternary` metadata paid off in `propagate()`, switching `minimize_learnt()` to branch on `reason_clause.ternary` before the binary path might shave enough reason-shape bookkeeping to matter without changing solver semantics.
- Files changed: `agent_log.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `next_steps.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, and the current solver files; refreshed `cProfile` on `large/test_6.cnf` with `python -m cProfile -s tottime satsolver.py large/test_6.cnf /tmp/current_main_profile_after_ternaryflag.txt`; confirmed the promoted main wrapper still beat `satsolver_fast.py` on the same-day SAT-heavy root-hit and mixed nine-case exact-CLI slices; created scratch candidates `scratch_satsolver_core_minreasonternary.py` and `scratch_satsolver_minreasonternary.py`; patched the scratch core so `minimize_learnt()` read `reason_clause.ternary` instead of using `len(reason_lits)` to detect ternary reasons; `python -m py_compile scratch_satsolver_minreasonternary.py scratch_satsolver_core_minreasonternary.py`; SAT/UNSAT smoke checks plus checker validation on `small/test_1.cnf` and `special/tseitin.cnf`; exact-CLI A/B of retained `satsolver.py` vs the scratch candidate on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; exact-CLI A/B on the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py --repeat 2`; same-day repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case baseline-vs-candidate runs via `benchmark_suite.py` to `/tmp/minreasonternary_{baseline,candidate}_cli_repeat2.txt`; deleted the scratch files after the full-suite gate regressed.
- Benchmark results: the branch looked very strong on the mixed exact-CLI hotspot gate, improving the two-order average from `24.7815s` to `23.3213s`, with especially large forward-order help on `special/hard.cnf` (`10.2945s -> 7.9395s`) and smaller wins on `large/test_6.cnf` (`11.5365s -> 11.2534s` forward, `11.3723s -> 11.1150s` reverse). But the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice regressed from `0.3652s` to `0.3889s`, and the real repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case suite regressed from `26.5996s` to `27.9836s`, still `59/59` correct. The biggest full-suite damage was `large/test_6.cnf`, which worsened from `11.5792s` to `13.0787s`, while `large/test_8.cnf` only improved slightly (`0.2730s -> 0.2651s`) and `special/hard.cnf` was basically flat-to-slightly better (`7.8688s -> 7.8196s`). So the hotspot gain did not generalize.
- Decision taken: discard the ternary-reason metadata branch and keep the retained solver files unchanged.
- Next recommended step: treat immutable clause-shape metadata as a still-live lane only when the win survives the full suite. The cached ternary flag paid off in `propagate()`, but reusing the same flag inside `minimize_learnt()` overfit the mixed hotspot slice and lost the repeat-aware exact-CLI corpus. Future conflict-analysis metadata work still needs same-day full-suite confirmation, not just a hotspot win.

## 2026-03-21T04:24:41+01:00 - Rejected a later `reduce_database()` schedule

- Objective: test whether reducing learnt-clause cleanup frequency, instead of increasing it, can help the dense UNSAT-heavy exact-CLI bottlenecks without introducing a new retention classifier.
- Hypothesis: because “reduce earlier” already failed hard and `reduce_database()` itself is not a large direct runtime hotspot, moving the post-reduction schedule from `1.5x` to `1.75x` might preserve more useful learnt clauses on `large/test_6.cnf` and `special/hard.cnf` while leaving the rest of the solver unchanged.
- Files changed: `agent_log.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `next_steps.md`, and `benchmark_summary.md`; refreshed `cProfile` on `special/hard.cnf` with `python -m cProfile -s tottime satsolver.py special/hard.cnf /tmp/current_cycle_profile_hard.txt`; created scratch candidates `scratch_satsolver_core_laterreduce.py` and `scratch_satsolver_laterreduce.py`; changed only the post-reduction `next_reduce` multiplier from `1.5` to `1.75`; `python -m py_compile scratch_satsolver_laterreduce.py scratch_satsolver_core_laterreduce.py`; SAT/UNSAT smoke checks plus checker validation on `small/test_1.cnf` and `special/tseitin.cnf`; exact-CLI A/B of retained `satsolver.py` vs the scratch candidate on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; deleted the scratch files after the candidate failed the first gate.

The two-order average regressed from `23.5526s` to `33.5169s`. `large/test_8.cnf` was catastrophic in both orders (`0.2912s -> 6.6520s` forward, `0.2766s -> 6.4384s` reverse), and `large/test_6.cnf` also regressed heavily (`11.5209s -> 15.2828s` forward, `11.3521s -> 15.4253s` reverse). `special/hard.cnf` improved in reverse (`7.8335s -> 6.0730s`) but still lost overall because the other cases gave that gain back several times over.
- Decision taken: discard the later `reduce_database()` schedule and keep the retained solver files unchanged.
- Next recommended step: treat schedule-only learnt-database changes as increasingly low-value unless a future branch brings a materially better classifier than just changing the post-reduction multiplier. The current evidence now says both “reduce earlier” and “reduce later” are dangerous on the exact-CLI hotspot mix.

## 2026-03-21T04:34:18+01:00 - Rejected a quaternary watched-clause fast path in `propagate()`

- Objective: test whether the next immutable clause-shape step after the kept `Clause.ternary` flag, an exact-4-literal family flag plus a dedicated watched-clause branch in `propagate()`, can produce another solver-core win on the exact-CLI path.
- Hypothesis: because the cached ternary family flag paid off in `propagate()` and fixed-size watched-clause families are still the cleanest remaining hot-loop specialization lane, adding `Clause.quaternary` plus a dedicated 4-literal replacement branch might reduce generic watcher-scan overhead without changing solver semantics.
- Files changed: `agent_log.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `next_steps.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, and the current `satsolver_core.py` hot path; created scratch candidates `scratch_satsolver_core_quaternaryflag.py` and `scratch_satsolver_quaternaryflag.py`; patched the scratch core to add immutable `Clause.quaternary` metadata and a dedicated exact-4-literal watched-clause branch in `propagate()`; `python -m py_compile scratch_satsolver_quaternaryflag.py scratch_satsolver_core_quaternaryflag.py`; SAT/UNSAT smoke checks plus checker validation on `small/test_1.cnf` and `special/tseitin.cnf`; exact-CLI A/B of retained `satsolver.py` vs the scratch candidate on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; exact-CLI A/B on the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py --repeat 2`; same-day repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case baseline-vs-candidate runs via `benchmark_suite.py` to `/tmp/quaternaryflag_{baseline,candidate}_cli_repeat2.txt`; deleted the scratch files after the full-suite gate regressed.
- Benchmark results: the branch cleared the mixed hotspot gate but failed the broader exact-CLI gates. On the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice, the two-order average improved from `23.8342s` to `23.6216s`, with small wins on `large/test_6.cnf` (`11.7722s -> 11.6805s` two-order average), `medium/test_4.cnf` (`1.6946s -> 1.6245s`), and `large/test_10.cnf` (`1.5582s -> 1.5424s`), while `special/hard.cnf` stayed roughly flat (`7.7618s -> 7.7718s`). But the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice regressed from `0.3453s` to `0.3528s`, and the real repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case suite regressed from `26.8303s` to `27.4920s`, still `59/59` correct. The main broad-suite damage was `large/test_6.cnf` (`11.7643s -> 12.2309s`), with smaller losses on `medium/test_4.cnf` (`1.6916s -> 1.7577s`), `large/test_10.cnf` (`1.5759s -> 1.6618s`), and `large/test_8.cnf` (`0.2603s -> 0.2729s`).
- Decision taken: discard the quaternary watched-clause branch and keep the retained solver files unchanged.
- Next recommended step: treat fixed-size watched-clause specialization beyond ternary as another likely propagation dead end unless a future branch shows a materially stronger broad exact-CLI win. The quaternary branch behaved like a classic hotspot overfit: it helped the mixed slice modestly, but the startup-sensitive SAT cases and the full repeat-aware suite still moved the wrong way overall.

## 2026-03-21T04:41:09+01:00 - Rejected local `append` / `pop` aliasing in `propagate()`

- Objective: test whether localizing the hottest list method lookups inside `propagate()` can turn the large `append` / `pop` cProfile cost into a measurable exact-CLI speedup without changing any solver logic.
- Hypothesis: because `propagate()` still dominates runtime and raw list methods are among the largest remaining Python-level costs on the dense bottlenecks, replacing repeated `trail.append(...)` and per-watcher-list `watchers.pop()` lookups with local aliases might shave enough pure overhead to matter while preserving the same search path.
- Files changed: `agent_log.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `next_steps.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, and the current `satsolver_core.py` hot path; refreshed `cProfile` on `large/test_6.cnf` and `special/hard.cnf`; created scratch candidates `scratch_satsolver_core_propagatelocals.py` and `scratch_satsolver_propagatelocals.py`; patched only `propagate()` to use local `trail_append` and per-watcher-list `watchers_pop` aliases; `python -m py_compile scratch_satsolver_propagatelocals.py scratch_satsolver_core_propagatelocals.py`; SAT/UNSAT smoke checks plus checker validation on `small/test_1.cnf` and `special/tseitin.cnf`; exact-CLI A/B of retained `satsolver.py` vs the scratch candidate on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; deleted the scratch files after the candidate lost the first gate.
- Benchmark results: the branch split sharply by order and did not clear the real mixed gate. Forward order improved from `25.1835s` to `24.5555s`, driven mostly by `medium/test_3.cnf` (`1.3872s -> 1.1335s`) and `medium/test_4.cnf` (`2.8343s -> 1.8080s`), but the denser bottlenecks already showed trouble there (`large/test_6.cnf 11.2736s -> 11.7422s`, `special/hard.cnf 7.7105s -> 7.8894s`). Reverse order then regressed from `23.1279s` to `23.8686s`, with `large/test_6.cnf` worsening again (`11.2801s -> 11.6826s`) and the medium cases losing their forward-order gains. So the two-order average still regressed from `24.1557s` to `24.2120s`, which is too weak and unstable to justify broader benchmarking.
- Decision taken: discard the local aliasing branch and keep the retained solver files unchanged.
- Next recommended step: treat tiny list-method lookup cleanup inside `propagate()` as another likely micro-optimization dead end unless a future branch removes more structural watcher/trail work than just localizing `append()` / `pop()`. The profiler is still right that these calls are expensive, but this branch shows that simply rebinding them is not enough to buy a stable end-to-end win.

## 2026-03-21T04:54:48+01:00 - Kept an import-gated API wrapper for CLI execution

- Objective: reduce `satsolver.py` exact-CLI startup overhead without losing the broad import-time API and helper surface that current tests and tooling rely on.
- Hypothesis: because the current main wrapper still lagged the leaner alternate wrapper on the same-day exact-CLI slices, building the broad alias surface and the root-pure-enabled `solve_cnf_serial()` helper only in import mode may narrow that gap while keeping the CLI path on direct `satsolver_core` calls.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py`, `out_cli_extended.txt`, `agent_log.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `next_steps.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, and the current `agent_log.md`; confirmed the retained main wrapper still trailed `satsolver_fast.py` on the same-day 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit exact-CLI slice and the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice; created scratch candidate `scratch_satsolver_importgated.py`; patched it so CLI mode only imports `sys` and `satsolver_core as base`, while import mode still exposes the broad alias surface plus `solve_cnf_serial()`; changed the CLI-path helpers to use `base.*` directly; `python -m py_compile scratch_satsolver_importgated.py`; SAT/UNSAT smoke checks plus checker validation on `small/test_1.cnf` and `special/tseitin.cnf`; exact-CLI A/B of retained `satsolver.py` vs the scratch candidate on the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py --repeat 2`; exact-CLI A/B on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; same-day repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case baseline-vs-candidate runs via `benchmark_suite.py` to `/tmp/importgated_{baseline,candidate}_cli_repeat2.txt`; merged the change into tracked `satsolver.py`; deleted the scratch file; `python -m py_compile satsolver.py satsolver_core.py satsolver_fast.py`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; SAT/UNSAT checker smoke checks on the retained main wrapper; refreshed `out_cli_extended.txt` with `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_cli_extended.txt ... --cli-script satsolver.py --repeat 2`.
- Benchmark results: the import-gated wrapper cleared all three exact-CLI gates. On the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice, the two-order average improved from `0.3905s` to `0.3842s`. On the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice, the two-order average improved much more strongly from `25.2601s` to `23.5388s`. On the same-day repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case baseline-vs-candidate comparison, the representative total improved from `27.6064s` to `27.5425s`, still `59/59` correct. After promotion into the tracked main wrapper, the refreshed retained artifact in `out_cli_extended.txt` landed at `26.4762s` representative, `52.9525s` measured, `53.2029s` wall clock, also `59/59` correct.
- Decision taken: keep the import-gated wrapper in `satsolver.py`, keep the retained CLI artifact update, and treat explicit CLI-mode/import-mode separation as a live exact-CLI wrapper lane.
- Next recommended step: future wrapper/startup work should prefer the explicit “lean when run as a script, rich when imported” split over lazy export forwarding or blanket alias trimming, but it should still require same-day repeat-aware full-suite confirmation because the temp baseline-vs-candidate full-suite edge here (`27.6064s -> 27.5425s`) was modest even though the refreshed retained artifact came out much stronger.

## 2026-03-21T05:03:29+01:00 - Rejected import-gating the standalone CLI surface in `satsolver_core.py`

## 2026-03-21T05:30:38+01:00 - Kept a low-density ternary root-pure gate on the fast exact-CLI path

- Objective: test whether root pure-literal presolve can come back as a useful exact-CLI lever if it is only enabled on formulas that match the current easy-hit family instead of on the dense pure-3-SAT hotspot families that previously punished the extra front-loaded pass.
- Hypothesis: because all current root-pure hits in the benchmark corpus are low-density all-3-SAT instances while the hardest exact-CLI hotspots are much denser, running iterative root-pure presolve only when all clauses are length `3` and clause density is at most `3.2` may recover the easy-case wins without paying the dense-case overhead that made earlier root-pure gates lose.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py`, `tests/test_solver_regressions.py`, `out_cli_extended.txt`, `agent_log.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `next_steps.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, and the current solver files; used quick corpus scripts to confirm that the current root-pure hits are low-density all-3-SAT cases while the dense hotspot families are not; created scratch candidate `scratch_satsolver_densityroot.py`; patched only the fast exact-CLI path to call iterative root-pure presolve when all clauses are length `3` and density is at most `3.2`; `python -m py_compile scratch_satsolver_densityroot.py`; SAT/UNSAT smoke checks plus checker validation on `small/test_1.cnf` and `special/tseitin.cnf`; exact-CLI A/B of retained `satsolver.py` vs the scratch candidate on the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py --repeat 2`; exact-CLI A/B on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; same-day repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case baseline-vs-candidate runs via `benchmark_suite.py` to `/tmp/densityroot_{baseline,candidate}_cli_repeat2.txt`; merged the gated path into tracked `satsolver.py`; added a regression covering the gate in `tests/test_solver_regressions.py`; `python -m py_compile satsolver.py satsolver_core.py satsolver_fast.py tests/test_solver_regressions.py`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; SAT/UNSAT checker smoke checks on the retained main wrapper; refreshed `out_cli_extended.txt` with `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_cli_extended.txt ... --cli-script satsolver.py --repeat 2`; deleted the scratch file after promotion.
- Benchmark results: the new gate stayed positive on every direct same-day A/B used for the keep. On the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit exact-CLI slice, the two-order average improved from `0.3794s` to `0.3674s`. On the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice, the two-order average also improved from `23.5744s` to `23.0621s`, with especially strong help on `large/test_6.cnf` and `small/test_10.cnf` while `special/hard.cnf` stayed roughly flat overall. The same-day repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case baseline-vs-candidate comparison improved from `26.1422s` to `25.9837s`, still `59/59` correct. After promotion into the tracked main wrapper, the refreshed retained artifact in `out_cli_extended.txt` landed at `26.1805s` representative, `52.3610s` measured, `52.5982s` wall clock, also `59/59` correct, which is not a new best versus the retained historical `25.7027s`.
- Decision taken: keep the low-density all-3-SAT root-pure gate on `solve_cnf_fast_serial()` in `satsolver.py`, keep the refreshed `out_cli_extended.txt`, and treat this as a cautious exact-CLI keep backed by same-day A/B rather than by the refreshed artifact alone.
- Next recommended step: root-pure work is live again only when a cheap prefilter avoids the dense 3-SAT hotspots. Future presolve tuning should keep using broad exact-CLI baseline-vs-candidate comparisons as the real gate and should not trust one refreshed retained artifact by itself when the margin is sub-percent.

- Hypothesis: because current `satsolver_fast.py` beat the retained `satsolver.py` on the same-day root-hit exact-CLI slice (`0.5025s -> 0.4947s`), the mixed nine-case hotspot slice (`32.7270s -> 32.5444s`), and the same-day repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case suite (`39.8399s -> 39.1591s`), removing the fast-path root-pure presolve from the current import-gated main wrapper may recover that win while keeping the richer import-time compatibility surface and test API in `satsolver.py`.
- Files changed: `satsolver.py`, `tests/test_solver_regressions.py`, `out_cli_extended.txt`, `agent_log.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `experiments.jsonl`, `next_steps.md`
- Tests executed: reread `autonomous_sat_agent_prompt.md`, `next_steps.md`, `benchmark_summary.md`, `satsolver.py`, and `satsolver_fast.py`; exact-CLI A/B of retained `satsolver.py` vs current `satsolver_fast.py` on the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py --repeat 2`; exact-CLI A/B on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; same-day repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case runs for retained `satsolver.py` and current `satsolver_fast.py` via `benchmark_suite.py` to `/tmp/cycle_{baseline,fast}_cli_repeat2.txt`; created scratch candidate `scratch_satsolver_noroot.py`; removed only the fast-path root-pure block from `solve_cnf_fast_serial()`; `python -m py_compile scratch_satsolver_noroot.py`; SAT/UNSAT smoke checks plus checker validation on `small/test_1.cnf` and `special/tseitin.cnf`; exact-CLI A/B of retained `satsolver.py` vs the scratch candidate on the 12-case SAT-heavy root-hit slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py --repeat 2`; exact-CLI A/B on the mixed nine-case hotspot slice via `tools/hotspot_compare.py`; same-day repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case candidate run via `benchmark_suite.py` to `/tmp/cycle_noroot_cli_repeat2.txt`; merged the change into tracked `satsolver.py`; replaced the old fast-root-pure regression with a direct “fast serial path skips root-pure presolve” regression in `tests/test_solver_regressions.py`; `python -m py_compile satsolver.py satsolver_core.py satsolver_fast.py tests/test_solver_regressions.py`; `python -m unittest discover -s tests -v`; SAT/UNSAT checker smoke checks on the retained main wrapper; refreshed `out_cli_extended.txt` with `python benchmark_suite.py satsolver out_cli_extended.txt ... --cli-script satsolver.py --repeat 2`; deleted the scratch file after promotion.
- Benchmark results: the current alternate file already pointed the lane in this direction: `satsolver_fast.py` beat retained `satsolver.py` on the same-day root-hit exact-CLI slice (`0.5025s -> 0.4947s`), the mixed nine-case hotspot slice (`32.7270s -> 32.5444s`), and the repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case suite (`39.8399s -> 39.1591s`), all `59/59` correct. The scratch main-wrapper no-root-pure candidate then improved the root-hit slice from `0.4790s` to `0.4603s` and improved the mixed nine-case exact-CLI hotspot slice from `31.9046s` to `31.4055s`. On the same-day repeat-aware exact-CLI 59-case suite, it improved strongly from `39.8399s` to `36.6465s`, still `59/59` correct, and also beat the same-day `satsolver_fast.py` rerun (`39.1591s`). After promotion into tracked `satsolver.py`, the refreshed retained artifact in `out_cli_extended.txt` landed at `38.0602s` representative, `76.1203s` measured, `76.3611s` wall clock, also `59/59` correct. That refreshed artifact is not a new historical best against the retained `25.7027s`, but the same-day baseline-vs-candidate comparison is large enough to keep decisively.
- Decision taken: keep the no-root-pure fast solve path in `satsolver.py`, update the regression to lock that fast-path policy directly, refresh `out_cli_extended.txt`, and treat this as the new exact-CLI baseline.
- Next recommended step: root-pure work on the main exact-CLI path is back to “off by default” status. Future presolve tuning should treat the current no-root-pure fast path as the baseline again and only revisit root-pure on the submission path with a materially stronger classifier than density alone plus same-day broad-suite confirmation.
